Logarithmic prismatic cohomology, motivic sheaves, and comparison theorems Thanks: F.B. is partially supported by the PRIN 2022 “The arithmetic of motives and L-functions”. A.M. is supported by Horizon Europe’s Marie Skłodowska-Curie Action PF 101103309 “MIPAC”, D.P. and T.L. were partially supported by the research training group GRK 2240 “Algebro-Geometric methods in Algebra, Arithmetic and Topology.”
Federico Binda, Tommy Lundemo, Alberto Merici, Doosung Park

Abstract
. We prove that (logarithmic) prismatic and (logarithmic) syntomic cohomology are representable in the category of logarithmic motives. As an application, we obtain Gysin maps for prismatic and syntomic cohomology, and we explicitly identify their cofibers. We also prove a smooth blow-up formula and we compute prismatic and syntomic cohomology of Grassmannians. In the second part of the paper, we develop a descent technique inspired by the work of Nizioł on log K K -theory. Using the resulting saturated descent , we prove de Rham and crystalline comparison theorems for log prismatic cohomology, and the existence of Gysin maps for A inf A_{\inf} -cohomology.



1.
Introduction

Let us fix a prime
p
. Prismatic cohomology is a cohomology theory for
p
-adic formal schemes introduced by Bhatt and Scholze
[
BS22
]
, building on the work of Bhatt, Morrow, and Scholze
[
BMS18
]
,
[
BMS19
]
. Classical
p
-adic cohomology theories, such as étale, de Rham, and crystalline cohomology, are all specializations of prismatic cohomology.

There are several approaches to prismatic cohomology. The construction in
[
BS22
]
uses a mixed-characteristic refinement of the crystalline site, based on the notion of
prism
; a sort of “deperfection” of (mixed characteristic) perfectoid rings. Given a prism
(A,I)
, the prismatic cohomology of a
p
-adic smooth formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
over
A/I
is then defined as
R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/A):=R\Gamma((\mathfrak{X}/A)_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
, where
(\mathfrak{X}/A)_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
is the prismatic site of
\mathfrak{X}
relative to
(A,I)
, and
\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
is the structure sheaf. It comes equipped with a decreasing filtration
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq*}
, the
Nygaard filtration
, and a Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi
. In general, prismatic cohomology is not complete with respect to the Nygaard filtration. The Nygaard completion of prismatic cohomology can be described in purely homotopy-theoretic terms, using trace invariants such as topological Hochschild homology. This is the approach of
[
BMS19
]
, and it is compared with that of Bhatt–Scholze in
[
BS22
, Theorem 13.1]
. This point of view has lent itself to numerous striking results in homotopy theory: let us highlight the computations in algebraic
K
-theory of Antieau–Krause–Nikolaus
[
AKN
]
and the generalization of prismatic cohomology to commutative ring spectra of Hahn–Raksit–Wilson
[
HRW22
]
.

The theory of prismatic cohomology in the sense of
[
BS22
]
admits a natural extension to formal schemes
\mathfrak{X}
that are not necessarily smooth over
A/I
, but merely smooth in the sense of
logarithmic geometry
. A logarithmic version of the prismatic site has been defined by Koshikawa
[
Kos22
]
and further developed by Koshikawa and Yao
[
KY23
]
, generalizing the comparison theorems of
[
BS22
]
to the semi-stable setting.

In
[
BLPØ23b
]
, the first, second, and fourth named authors, together with Østvær, defined a variant of logarithmic prismatic cohomology based on the approach of
[
BMS19
]
. The role of topological Hochschild homology is played by Rognes’
logarithmic
topological Hochschild homology
[
Rog09
]
. The resulting theory
R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)
is called the (absolute) Nygaard-complete logarithmic prismatic cohomology. It is a (strict) étale sheaf on the category of quasisyntomic log formal schemes and comes equipped with a Nygaard filtration and a Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi
. We may then define log syntomic cohomology analogously to the non-log variant of
[
BMS19
]
, namely as the equalizer

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\rm Fsyn}(-,\mathbb{Z}_{p}(i)):=\mathrm{eq}\Bigl(\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq i}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}\Bigr)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

of the Frobenius and the canonical map.

This paper is dedicated to further study of log syntomic cohomology and Nygaard-complete log prismatic cohomology. We exhibit these as theories defined on a suitable category of mixed motives and prove comparison theorems analogous to those of
[
BMS19
]
. Our results are independent of
[
Kos22
]
,
[
KY23
]
, as a log analog of the comparison result
[
BS22
, Theorem 13.1]
is still not available.
We remark, however, that as in
[
BMS19
]
it is possible to construct (depending on the choice of a perfectoid base
R
) a non-Nygaard completed version of log prismatic cohomology out of the completed version, by left Kan extension from the log smooth case. Our results also apply to the resulting theory
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{-/R}
.

We will now give an overview of the main results of this work and discuss the methods used to achieve them.

1.1.
Motivic realizations

Let us fix a base (formal) quasisyntomic
p
-adic scheme
S
. Crystalline cohomology, Hodge cohomology, prismatic cohomology, and syntomic cohomology are all examples of cohomology theories that fail to be
{\mathbf{A}}^{1}
-homotopy invariant (for prismatic cohomology this indeed follows from the crystalline comparison theorem, and for syntomic cohomology follows from e.g.
[
BMS19
, Theorem 1.12(5)]
, together with the fact that
\operatorname{TC}
is not
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariant).
In particular, the functors

R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\text{ and }R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(-,\mathbb{Z}_{p})\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{QSyn}_{S},\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))

do not factor through the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{FDA}^{\rm eff}(S;\mathbb{Z}_{p})
of (effective, formal, étale) motivic sheaves of Ayoub, Morel, Voevodsky and others, defined as the subcategory of
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-local objects of the category of étale sheaves of complexes of
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-modules on smooth formal
S
-schemes. This category is a powerful tool for studying
rational

p
-adic cohomology theories. For example, it can be used to deduce finiteness results
[
Vez18
]
, to define categories of coefficients
[
DN18
]
, Gysin triangles
[
Dég08
]
, and to study in large generality monodromy operators and weight filtrations
[
BKV22
]
,
[
BGV23
]
.

By enlarging the framework to the category of logarithmic schemes, we gain an extra level of flexibility that not only admits more general objects (such as formal models with semi-stable reduction) but also allows a different motivic formalism to be applied. Namely, we can work in the category of logarithmic motivic sheaves introduced in
[
BPØ22b
]
,
[
BPØ22a
]
,
[
BPØ23
]
. Its construction mimics Voevodsky’s definition; one starts with the category of strict étale or Nisnevich sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
or
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{sNis}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
on the category of fs log smooth log schemes separated of finite type over a base
S
, for a ring of coefficients
\Lambda
. One then considers the localization with respect to the projections
(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{S},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{S})\times_{S}X\to X
for every
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S}
and
n\geq 1
.
The effect of inverting this class of maps is striking, as it recovers several results that were previously only available in the
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-local setting, notably the construction of Gysin sequences and a Thom space isomorphism for Chern-oriented theories. By passing to
\mathbf{P}^{1}
-spectra one obtains a non-effective version,
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda)
, which is our preferred framework in this text. A verbatim translation of the above construction allows for
p
-adic formal log schemes as input. We denote the resulting category
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\Lambda)
or
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
.

In the first part of the paper, our main goal is to prove the representability of the aforementioned cohomology theories in the log motivic categories of formal schemes. Theorem
3.15
gives oriented motivic spectra

\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})),\quad\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\widehat{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))

for any quasi-compact and quasi-separated
p
-adic formal base scheme
S
(with trivial log structure). If
S\in\mathrm{QSyn}_{R}
for a perfectoid ring
R
, we also obtain oriented motivic spectra
\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}
from the non-Nygaard complete versions. Both
\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
are
E_{\infty}
-rings, and there are functorial ring maps induced by the divided Frobenius and the canonical morphism in prismatic cohomology. Forming their equalizer, we then obtain a motivic ring spectrum
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}}
, representing log syntomic cohomology. The orientation is induced by the existence of Chern classes, due to the work of Bhatt–Lurie
[
BL22
]
.
Similarly, building on our previous work
[
BLPØ23b
]
,
[
BLPØ23a
]
,
[
Mer22
]
, we can construct motivic ring spectra
\mathbf{E}^{\rm Hdg}
,
\mathbf{E}^{\rm dR}
, representing Hodge and de Rham cohomology for log smooth formal
S
-schemes, as well as
\mathbf{E}^{\rm dRW}
,
\mathbf{E}^{\rm crys}
representing de Rham-Witt and log crystalline cohomology over a perfect field
k
of characteristic
p
(with trivial log structure). See §
2.4
.

This has as an immediate application the following
result (see Theorem
3.17
):

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}
. Let
X
be a smooth scheme over
S
and
Z\subseteq X
a closed subscheme of relative codimension
d
, such that
Z\to X\to S
is smooth. Let
{\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)
denote the blow-up of
X
in
Z
and
E
be the exceptional divisor, so that
({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{S}
. Then for all
j
there are functorial Gysin maps in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\mathrm{gys}_{Z/X}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\colon

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(Z_{p}^{\wedge})\{j-d\}[-2d]\to R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X_{p}^{\wedge})\{j\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{gys}_{Z/X}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\colon

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j-d}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(Z_{p}^{\wedge})\{j-d\}[-2d]\to\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X_{p}^{\wedge})\{j\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{gys}_{Z/X}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}}\colon

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(Z_{p}^{\wedge},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j-d))[-2d]\to R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(X_{p}^{\wedge},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j))

whose homotopy cofibers are respectively given as

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge})\{j\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge})\{j\}

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j)).

If
S\in\mathrm{QSyn}_{R}
with
R
perfectoid, we have similar Gysin maps for the non-completed prismatic cohomology of
[
BS22
]
, relative to the perfect prism
(A_{\inf}(R),\mathrm{ker}(\theta))
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{gys}_{Z/X}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\colon

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(Z_{p}^{\wedge}/A_{\inf}(R))\{j-d\}[-2d]\to R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{p}^{\wedge}/A_{\inf}(R))\{j\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{gys}_{Z/X}^{\operatorname{Fil}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\colon

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j-d}R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(Z_{p}^{\wedge}/A_{\inf}(R))\{j-d\}[-2d]\to\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{p}^{\wedge}/A_{\inf}(R))\{j\}.

The homotopy cofibers are respectively given as

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge}/R)\{j\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge}/R)\{j\}

In particular, not only we can define a push-forward for (non log) prismatic or syntomic cohomology along the inclusion of a smooth subscheme
Z\subset X
, but we can also identify the cofiber of this map in terms of an explicit
logarithmic
cohomology theory. Coherently with the philosophy sketched in
[
BLPØ23a
]
, we might call the corresponding bifiber sequence the
residue sequence
for prismatic (resp. syntomic) cohomology.

The Gysin map described above appears after a trivialization of the motivic Thom space of the normal bundle of
Z
in
X
, which is possible in light of the orientation on prismatic cohomology. This is an analog of the homotopy purity theorem of Morel and Voevodsky
[
MV99
, Theorem 2.23]
, proved in
[
BPØ22b
, §7.4]
,
[
BPØ23
]
. In the
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-local setting, the logarithmic term would be replaced by the open complement
U=X-Z
: we interpret the pair
({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)
as a relative compactification of
U
, which preserves the homotopy type. Note that more general Gysin maps (where also
X
and
Z
are allowed to have a non-trivial log structure) can be constructed, using
[
BPØ22b
, Theorem 7.5.4]
. See Remark
3.19
for more details.

Another byproduct of the representability result of prismatic and syntomic cohomology is a computation of the prismatic and syntomic cohomology of the Grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)
for every
r,n
. See Theorem
3.21
for the precise notation.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}
. Let
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{S}
. There are isomorphisms of bigraded rings, functorial in
X
:

\displaystyle\varphi_{r,n}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\colon H^{*}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X^{\wedge}_{p})\{\bullet\}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}R_{r,n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{*}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}((\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\times X)_{p}^{\wedge})\{\bullet\}

\displaystyle\varphi_{r,n}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\colon\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}H^{*}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X^{\wedge}_{p})\{\bullet\}\otimes{Z}R_{r,n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}H^{*}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}((\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\times X)_{p}^{\wedge})\{\bullet\}

\displaystyle\varphi_{r,n}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}}\colon H^{*}_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(X^{\wedge}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\bullet))\otimes{Z}R_{r,n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{*}_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}((\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\times X)_{p}^{\wedge},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\bullet)).

If
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}_{R}
with
R
perfectoid, a similar statement holds for the non-completed prismatic cohomology relative to
R
.

Yet another application of our general motivic formalism, we obtain blow-up formulas for syntomic and prismatic cohomology, see Theorem
3.22
.

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}
. Let
X
be a smooth scheme over
S
and let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subscheme of pure codimension
d
such that
Z\to X\to S
is smooth. Let
X^{\prime}={\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)
. Then there are equivalences, functorial in
(X,Z)
,

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X^{\wedge}_{p})\{j\}\oplus\bigoplus_{0<i<d}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(Z^{\wedge}_{p})\{j-i\}[-2i]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}((X^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{p})\{j\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X^{\wedge}_{p})\{j\}\oplus\bigoplus_{0<i<d}\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq j-i}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(Z^{\wedge}_{p})\{j-i\}[-2i]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\{j\}((X^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{p})

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(X^{\wedge}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j))\oplus\bigoplus_{0<i<d}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(Z^{\wedge}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j-i))[-2i]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}((X^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j))

If
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}_{R}
with
R
perfectoid, a similar statement holds for the non-completed prismatic cohomology relative to
R
.

The previous result was first proved in
[
BL22
, Corollary 9.4.2]
for syntomic cohomology, with the extra assumption that
Z
is the image of a section
s\colon Y\to X
of a smooth morphism
f\colon X\to Y
.

Let us briefly sketch how one establishes the representability of prismatic cohomology. The first step is to prove that it is
(\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})
-invariant for every
n\geq 1
. Using quasisyntomic descent, we can reduce to the case where the base is a perfectoid ring
R
: in this case, the HKR-filtration
[
BLPØ23a
, Theorem 5.15]
induces as in
[
BMS19
]
a finite
secondary
filtration on the graded pieces with respect to the Nygaard filtration
\operatorname{gr}^{i}_{N}
, with graded quotients given by exterior powers of the Gabber’s logarithmic cotangent complex. It is explained in
[
BLPØ23a
, §8.3]
that this is
(\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})
-invariant.
The second step is
\mathbf{P}^{1}
-stability: we prove this by showing that the graded commutative monoid
(R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
admits a Chern orientation, and this will follow from the construction of the prismatic first Chern class of
[
BL22
]
in the non-log situation.

1.2.
Saturated descent and comparison theorems

In the second part of the paper, we concentrate on more explicit descriptions of log prismatic cohomology. Namely, we prove that it can be computed via descent of (non-logarithmic) prismatic cohomology in many cases of interest. The main application of this is a comparison result with log crystalline cohomology:

Theorem 1.4

(Theorems
4.30
and
4.31
)
.

Let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p
. For all
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(k,\mathrm{triv})}
and
Y\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(k,\mathbb{N})}
with
{\underline{Y}}
generically reduced, we have equivalences of filtered
E_{\infty}
-rings that depends functorially on
X
and
Y
:

R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X/k)\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(X/W(k))\quad R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(({\underline{Y}},\partial Y\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})/k)\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(Y/W(k,\mathbb{N})).

Here,
(k,\mathrm{triv})
(resp.
(k,\mathbb{N})
) denotes
\operatorname{Spec}(k)
with trivial (resp. with the standard log point) log structure.

We will reduce both statements to the ordinary, non-logarithmic crystalline comparison of
[
BMS19
]
. The central tool for this is
saturated descent
, a technique first considered in
[
Niz08
]
. For this, we form the Čech nerve in the category of saturated monoids and get for a monoid map
M\to N
a descent datum

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A,M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N],N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N\oplus_{M}^{\rm sat}N],N\oplus_{M}^{\rm sat}N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}

We prove that this datum induces the following descent property:

Theorem 1.5

(Theorem
4.18
and Example
4.19
)
.

Let
B
be a
p
-complete ring with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion. For
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{B}
, the log prismatic cohomology
R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X_{p}^{\wedge})
can be computed étale locally as limit of (non-log) prismatic cohomology

\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}({\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{B\langle M\rangle}B\langle M_{\rm perf}\oplus_{M}^{\rm sat}\ldots\oplus_{M}^{\rm sat}M_{\rm perf}\rangle})

where
(A,M)
is a strict chart on
X
.

We stress that the rings
A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{B\langle M\rangle}B\langle N\oplus_{M}^{\rm sat}\ldots\oplus_{M}^{\rm sat}N\rangle
are not obtained by tensor products in any category of rings: rather, these
saturated
tensor products actively extract data from the log structure.
By the same method, one obtains similar results for the log crystalline cohomology (see Theorems
4.25
and
4.27
), which combined with the crystalline comparison of
[
BMS19
]
gives Theorem
1.4
.

This descent datum is considered also in
[
KY23
, §6]
applied to the Kummer étale cohomology with
p
inverted, and in
loc. cit.
it is used to deduce a Kummer étale comparison isomorphism
[
KY23
, Theorem 6.1 and Proposition 6.13]
.

1.3.
A log version of Breuil-Kisin cohomology

In the last part of the paper, we use the above results to construct a logarithmic version of the Breuil–Kisin cohomology of
[
BMS19
, Theorem 1.2]
. For
K
a discrete valuation field with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, uniformizer
\varpi
and residue field
k
, let
\mathfrak{S}=W(k)\llbracket z\rrbracket
. There is a surjective map
\tilde{\theta}:\mathfrak{S}\to\mathcal{O}_{K}
determined by the inclusion
W(k)\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{K}
and
z\mapsto\varpi
. The kernel of this map is generated by an Eisenstein polynomial
E=E(z)\in\mathfrak{S}
for
\varpi
. Let
\varphi
be the endomorphism of
\mathfrak{S}
determined by the Frobenius on
W(k)
and
z\mapsto z^{p}
.

Theorem 1.6

(Theorems
6.1
and
6.2
)
.

Let
\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi)}^{\rm cart}
) be the category of fs log schemes of finite type that are log smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
(resp. log smooth over
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi)
of Cartier type). There are cohomology theories

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/\mathfrak{S})

\displaystyle\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathrm{triv})}^{\rm op}\to\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{S})

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/(\mathfrak{S},\varpi))

\displaystyle\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi)}^{\rm cart,op}\to\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{S})

with values in
(p,z)
-complete
E_{\infty}
-algebras together with a
\varphi
-linear Frobenius endomorphism having the following features:

(1)

(Breuil–Kisin) The Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi
induces equivalences

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/\mathfrak{S})\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\varphi}\mathfrak{S}[1/E]

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/\mathfrak{S})[1/E]

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/(\mathfrak{S},\varpi))\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\varphi}\mathfrak{S}[1/E]

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/\mathfrak{S})[1/E]

so the cohomology groups are Breuil–Kisin modules in the sense of
[
BMS19
, Definition 1.1]
.

(2)

(de Rham comparison) After scalar extension along
\theta=\tilde{\theta}\circ\varphi
, one recovers log de Rham cohomology:

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/\mathfrak{S})\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\theta}^{L}\mathcal{O}_{K}

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm dR}(-/\mathcal{O}_{K})

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/(\mathfrak{S},\varpi))\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\theta}^{L}\mathcal{O}_{K}

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm dR}(-/(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi))

(3)

(crystalline comparison) After scalar extension along the map
\mathfrak{S}\to W(k)
which is the Frobenius on
W(k)
and sends
z
to
0
, one recovers log crystalline cohomology of the special fiber:

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/\mathfrak{S})\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}^{L}W(k)

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}((-)_{k}/W(k))

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/(\mathfrak{S},\varpi))\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}^{L}W(k)

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}((-)_{(k,\mathbb{N})}/(W(k,\mathbb{N})))

The strategy of the construction of these cohomology theories follows
[
BMS19
, §11]
: they are defined in terms of relative log
\operatorname{THH}
over the log cyclotomic bases
(\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{N}])
and
(\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{N}],\mathbb{N})
, together with a Frobenius descent: this last part, in turns, follows from a logarithmic version of the Segal conjecture (see Proposition
5.1
and
5.2
).

Similarly to the absolute case, the cohomology theory
R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/\mathfrak{S})
is again representable in the log motivic category of formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, building an oriented ring spectrum (Theorem
6.5
):

\mathbf{E}^{\rm BK}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathfrak{S})).

This has the following interesting application (see Corollary
6.6
): let
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{Sm}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
be proper and let
Z\subseteq X
a regular closed immersion in
\operatorname{\mathrm{Sm}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
of relative dimension
d
. Let
C
be the completion of an algebraic closure of the
p
-adic completion of
K(p^{1/p^{\infty}})
. Then there is a functorial Gysin map

R\Gamma_{A_{\inf}}((Z_{p}^{\wedge})_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})\{j-d\}[-2d]\to R\Gamma_{A_{\inf}}((X_{p}^{\wedge})_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})\{j\},

where
R\Gamma_{A_{\inf}}
is the
A_{\inf}
-cohomology of
[
BMS18
]
, and whose homotopy cofiber is

R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge}/\mathfrak{S})\{j\}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}^{L}A_{\inf}.

Similarly, we have the computation of the cohomology of Grassmanians and the smooth blow-up formula. We remark that the Gysin or residue sequences considered here are compatible with the corresponding sequences for de Rham, Hodge, Hodge-Tate, and crystalline cohomology, via the comparison isomorphisms.

1.4.
Future perspectives

We would find it very appealing to understand under which conditions the Nygaard-complete variant of log prismatic cohomology considered here indeed comes to life as the Nygaard-completion of the site-theoretic definition pursued by Koshikawa–Yao. Such a comparison result would, among other things, open the door to pursue log variants of the global motivic filtration of Bhatt–Lurie
[
BL22
, §6.4]
. We consider this, in turn, to be a stepping stone to understanding the even filtration of Hahn–Raksit–Wilson in the setting of log ring spectra, and its compatibility with the residue sequences considered here.

In light of Theorem
4.18
, it is tempting to
define
log
K
-theory in terms of saturated descent. We intend to pursue this definition in future work and relate it to trace invariants based on Rognes’ log topological Hochschild homology. A similar definition of log
K
-theory is introduced by Nizioł
[
Niz08
, §4]
.

Notation

We will use freely the notation from the table in
[
BPØ23
, §1.1]
: in particular every (log) smooth morphism will always be considered separated and of finite type, unless specified. Log schemes in §
2
and
3
will always be fine and saturated, unless specified. The base of every category of (log) motives will always be assumed to be quasi-compact and quasi-separated, but not necessarily Noetherian.

For
\Lambda
a commutative ring and
\mathcal{C}
a
\Lambda
-linear st
able
\infty
-category, we let
\operatorname{map}_{\mathcal{C}}
be the mapping spectrum enriched in
\mathcal{D}(\Lambda)
, and
\operatorname{\underline{map}}_{\mathcal{C}}
the internal mapping spectrum of
\mathcal{C}
. If there is no confusion, we will suppress
\mathcal{C}
.

We will use the following convention regarding monoid rings: For any commutative monoid
M
and a fixed prime number
p
, we let
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\langle M\rangle
denote the
p
-completion of the monoid ring
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[M]
. For a different
p
-complete base ring
R
,
we shall write
R\langle M\rangle
for the
p
-complete tensor product
R\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\langle M\rangle
.

Finally, we will always consider Gabber’s log cotangent complex (
[
Ols05
, §8]
) and the corresponding version of log derived de Rham cohomology: we refer to
[
Bha12b
, §6]
for the construction and
[
BLPØ23a
, §3]
and
[
BLPØ23b
, §2]
for a quick recall. We will freely refer to
[
BLPØ23b
, §2 and 3]
for the results and constructions on logarithmic (topological) Hochschild homology, as introduced by Rognes
[
Rog09
]
.

2.
Log motives and abstract representability results

Throughout this section, we fix a
p
-complete ring
R
with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion (that is, the
p
-primary torsion is killed by a fixed power of
p
) and trivial log structure. This assumption is necessary to apply the motivic formalism developed in
[
BPØ22b
]
,
[
BPØ23
]
, but will be dropped in later sections where more general comparison results are considered.

2.1.
Motivic sheaves

We begin by recalling some facts about
p
-adic formal (log) schemes in the non (necessarily) locally-Noetherian setting. Log structures on formal schemes will always be defined étale locally. For locally Noetherian fs log schemes, this does not interfere with the setting of
[
BPØ22b
]
in light of Nizioł’s theorem
[
Niz06
, Theorem 5.6]
.

Definition 2.1
.

(see
[
Kos22
, §A.1]
)
A
bounded
p
-adic formal log scheme
over
R
is a pair consisting of a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme
{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}
(i.e. with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion) and a map of étale sheaves of monoids
\alpha\colon M_{\mathfrak{X}}\to\mathcal{O}_{{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}}
that induces an isomorphism
\alpha^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\times}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\times}
. For
(A,M)
a
p
-complete pre-log
R
-algebra such that
A
has bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion, we let
\operatorname{Spf}(A,M)^{a}
be the
p
-adic formal log scheme
\operatorname{Spf}(A)
with log structure given by the logification of the map
M\to\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{Spf}(A)}
, where
M
is the constant sheaf on the étale site of
\operatorname{Spf}(A)
.
A bounded
p
-adic formal log scheme is
quasi-coherent

[
Bei13
, §1.1]
if étale-locally it admits a chart, i.e. étale-locally on
{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}
it is isomorphic to
\operatorname{Spf}(A,M)^{a}
for
(A,M)
a
p
-complete pre-log
R
-algebra such that
A
has bounded
p^{\infty}
torsion. We say that
\mathfrak{X}
is
coherent
if it is quasi-coherent and if the charts
(A,M)
can be chosen with
\overline{M}=M/M^{*}
a finitely generated monoid.

Recall that for a commutative ring
B
, an object
M\in\mathcal{D}(B)
is called
p
-completely flat if
M\otimes^{L}_{B}B/pB\in\mathcal{D}(B/pB)
is concentrated in degree
0
and a flat
B/pB
-module. Similarly, a
B
-module
N
is
p
-completely flat if
N[0]\in\mathcal{D}(B)
is
p
-completely flat.

Definition 2.2
.

Let
(A,M)
be a pre-log
R
-algebra, and suppose that
A
is
p
-complete with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion. We say that
(A,M)
is
derived formally log smooth
if
R\langle M\rangle\to A
is
p
-completely flat and the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}
is
p
-completely flat. A quasi-coherent bounded
p
-adic formal log scheme
\mathfrak{X}
over
R
is
derived formally log smooth
if, strict étale locally, it is isomorphic to
\operatorname{Spf}(A,M)^{a}
with
(A,M)
derived formally log smooth. We let
\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R}
denote the category of quasi-coherent bounded
p
-adic formal log schemes derived formally log smooth over
R
. We equip
\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R}
with the strict étale topology, i.e.
\mathfrak{Y}\to\mathfrak{X}
is a strict étale cover if it is strict and
(|{\underline{\mathfrak{Y}}}|,\mathcal{O}_{{\underline{\mathfrak{Y}}}/p^{n}})\to(|{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}|,\mathcal{O}_{{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}/p^{n}})
is an étale cover for all
n
.

Remark 2.3
.

If
R
has bounded
p^{\infty}
torsion and
A
is
p
-completely flat over
R
, then
A
has bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion by
[
BMS19
, Corollary 4.8]
. Note that the log cotangent complex is logification-invariant by
[
BLPØ23a
, Lemma 3.11]
and satisfies strict étale descent, so if
(A,M)
is a quasismooth pre-log
R
-algebra in the sense of
[
BLPØ23b
, Definition 4.9]
such that every point has a chart
M^{\prime}\to A
that satisfies
R\langle M^{\prime}\rangle\to A
is
p
-completely flat, then étale locally on
A
we have
\mathbb{L}_{(A,(M^{\prime})^{a})}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)}
. This is
p
-completely flat, so
\operatorname{Spf}(A,M)^{a}
is derived formally log smooth over
R
.

Remark 2.4
.

The definition of formal smoothness that we have introduced above is slightly non-standard and is modeled on the notion of derived log smoothness of
[
SSV16
]
, used in
[
BLPØ23a
]
, with the extra condition that the map
R\langle M\rangle\to A
is
p
-completely flat. Note that if
\mathfrak{X}
is locally of finite presentation over
R
and if the log structure on
\mathfrak{X}
is fine, then a theorem of Kato
[
Kat88
, Theorem 3.5]
, together with
[
SSV16
, Theorem 6.4]
, gives, locally on
\mathfrak{X}
, charts with the property that
R\langle M\rangle\to A
is flat. We are mostly interested in the case of discrete (that is, underived) log schemes and log rings, and so we will drop the adjective “derived” and refer to the condition above as “formal smoothness”.

Remark 2.5
.

For
X
a
fine
log scheme with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion, we consider its formal
p
-completion as the bounded
p
-adic formal log scheme
X_{p}^{\wedge}
with
{\underline{X_{p}^{\wedge}}}={\underline{X}}\times\operatorname{Spf}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
with pullback log structure. If
X
is log smooth over
R
,
X_{p}^{\wedge}
is formally log smooth over
R
: indeed by
[
Ogu18
, Theorem IV.3.3.1]
, étale-locally on
{\underline{X}}
there is a chart
\operatorname{Spec}(A,M)
such that
R[M]\to A
is smooth. Since
R[M]
is a free
R
-module,
R[M]
has bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion, in particular by
[
BMS19
, Lemma 4.4 and 4.7]
, the (classic)
p
-completion
R\langle M\rangle=(R[M])_{p}^{\wedge}\to A_{p}^{\wedge}
is
p
-completely flat;
moreover, the cofiber sequence
[
BLPØ23a
, (3.4)]

A_{p}^{\wedge}\otimes_{A}^{L}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}\to\mathbb{L}_{(A_{p}^{\wedge},M)/R}\to\mathbb{L}_{(A_{p}^{\wedge},M)/(A,M)}

implies that after derived
p
-completion
\mathbb{L}_{(A_{p}^{\wedge},M)/R}\simeq A_{p}^{\wedge}\otimes_{A}^{L}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}
, which is
p
-completely flat as
\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}\simeq\Omega^{1}_{(A,M)/R}
by
[
BLPØ23a
, Propositions 4.5 and 4.6]
, which is a projective (hence flat)
R
-module. The assignment
X\mapsto X_{p}^{\wedge}
is clearly functorial and for any
\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r^{L,\otimes}}
it induces an adjoint pair:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{R},\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R},\mathcal{C})}

For
\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{R}\subseteq\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{R}
the category of log smooth schemes over
R
such that
{\underline{X}}
is smooth (see
[
BPØ22b
, A.5.10]
): the
p
-completion induces again

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{R},\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R},\mathcal{C})}

Definition 2.6
.

Let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. Let
(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{\operatorname{Spf}(R)},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{\operatorname{Spf}(R)})\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R}
be the formal completion of the log scheme
(\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})
. Then we let
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(R,\Lambda)
be the localization of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
with respect to the class maps given by the projections
(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{\operatorname{Spf}(R)},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{\operatorname{Spf}(R)})\times\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{X}

for
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R}
and
n\geq 1
. By construction, there is a canonical monoidal adjunction:

(2.6.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(R,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(R,\Lambda)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Comp}_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Comp}^{*}}

where
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(R,\Lambda)
is the
\infty
-category of algebraic (strict) étale log motivic sheaves constructed in the same way starting from
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{R},\mathcal{C})
(see
[
BPØ22b
, Proposition 5.4.2]
and
[
BPØ23
, Theorem 3.5.5 and 3.5.6]
for all the equivalent models).

2.2.
Generalities on spectra

We briefly review the process of formally inverting the tensor product with a fixed object of a symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-category. This material is not new, and probably well-known to the experts, but we collect the relevant results for the reader’s convenience. See
[
Rob15
]
for a detailed discussion.

Definition 2.7
.

Let
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
be the forgetful functor from symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories. By
[
Lur17
, Example 3.1.3.14]
it admits a left adjoint, denoted
\mathrm{Sym}^{\infty}(-)
. We
let
\mathrm{Sym}^{\infty}(*)
be the free symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category generated by one object
*
. This was denoted
free^{\otimes}(\Delta[0])
in
[
Rob15
, §2.1]
. Note that the underlying
\infty
-category of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\infty}(*)
agrees with
B\Sigma{N}=\coprod_{n}B\Sigma_{n}
(see also
[
AI23
, Construction 1.3.1]
).

Definition 2.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r^{L,\otimes}}
be a stable and presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, and let
\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma}=\mathrm{Fun}^{\rm L}(\mathrm{Sym}^{\infty}(*),\mathcal{C})^{\otimes}
be the category of symmetric sequences in
\mathcal{C}
as in
[
Lur17
, Definition 6.3.0.2]
(see
[
CD09
, Definition 6.3]
for the
1
-categorical version), equipped with the Day convolution product
[
Lur17
, §2.2.6]
.

We have
\mathrm{Fun}^{\rm L}(\mathrm{Sym}^{\infty}(*),\mathcal{C})_{\langle 1\rangle}=\prod_{n}\mathrm{Fun}^{\rm L}(B\Sigma_{n},\mathcal{C})
.
Informally, its objects are given by collections
(A_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, where each
A_{n}
is an object of
\mathcal{C}
equipped with an action of
\Sigma_{n}
.

Construction 2.9
.

We define an endofunctor
(-)\{-1\}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma}
as follows. Given
X\in\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma}
, we set
X\{-1\}_{0}=0
and
X\{-1\}_{n}=\Sigma_{n}\times_{\Sigma_{n-1}}X_{n-1}
, where
\Sigma_{n}\times_{\Sigma_{n-1}}X_{n-1}
is the equalizer

\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\Sigma_{n}}X_{n-1}\xrightarrow{(\tau_{\sigma}-\tau^{\prime}_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{n-1}}}\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\Sigma_{n}}X_{n-1}

and
\tau_{\sigma}
(resp.
\tau_{\sigma}^{\prime}
) denotes the endomorphism of
\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\Sigma_{n}}X_{n-1}
given by the action of
\Sigma_{n-1}\subset\Sigma_{n}
permuting the components (resp. given by the diagonal action). See
[
CD09
, §6.2]
.

Let
\mathbbm{1}\{-1\}\in\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma}
be given as
(\mathbbm{1}\{-1\})_{n}=\mathbbm{1}
if
n=1
and
0
otherwise, where
\mathbbm{1}
is the unit of
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
. By definition of the convolution product, we observe that
M\{-1\}=M\otimes\mathbbm{1}\{-1\}.

Remark 2.10
.

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the canonical projection induces an evaluation functor

\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma}\to\mathcal{C},\quad(A_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mapsto A_{n}

This functor has a left adjoint
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma}
, given by sending
X
to
X\{n\}=(-)\{1\}^{\circ n}X
, where by abuse of notation we write
X
for the image of
X
via

\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{Fun}^{L}(*,\mathcal{C})\to\prod_{n}\mathrm{Fun}^{L}(B\Sigma_{n},\mathcal{C})

given by left Kan extension along the maps
*\to B\Sigma_{n}
.

Recall from
[
Rob15
, Definition 2.16]
that an object
T
in a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
is called
symmetric
if the cyclic permutation
\sigma_{3}
on
T\otimes T\otimes T
is equivalent to the identity on
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 2.11
.

Let
T
be a symmetric object of
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
, and let
S_{T}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\infty}(T\{-1\})\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma})
be the free commutative algebra on
T\{-1\}
.
Following
[
Hov01
, Definition 6.2]
or
[
CD09
, §6.6]
, we define the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Pre}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{T}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C})
of symmetric
T
-pre-spectra in
\mathcal{C}
as the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Mod}_{S_{T}}(\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma})
.

For
M\in\operatorname{Mod}_{S_{T}}(\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma})
, we have the structure map
S_{T}\otimes M\to M
. Precomposing with the canonical morphism
T\{-1\}\to S_{T}
, we obtain a map

\sigma_{M}\colon T\{-1\}\otimes M\simeq(T\otimes M)\{-1\}\to M

where the tensor product is the convolution product in
\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma}
. By adjunction, we obtain a map

(2.11.1)

\gamma_{M}\colon M\to\underline{\operatorname{map}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma}}(T\{-1\},M)=:\Omega_{T}M.

In components, it is given by a collection of morphisms
M_{n}\to{\underline{\operatorname{map}}}_{\mathcal{C}}(T,M_{n+1})
for
n\geq 0
.

Definition 2.12

(
[
Hov01
, Definitions 7.6 and 7.7]
,
[
CD09
, §6.23]
)
.

The symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category of symmetric
T
-spectra
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{T}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C})
in
\mathcal{C}
is the Bousfield localization of the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Pre}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{T}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C})
of symmetric
T
-pre-spectra with respect to the collection of morphisms
\gamma_{M}
of (
2.11.1
) for
M\in\mathcal{C}^{\Sigma}
.

Proposition 2.13
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{T}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C})
is a presentable stable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, and it is naturally equivalent to the formal inversion
\mathcal{C}[T^{-1}]
of the object
T
constructed in
[
Rob15
, Proposition 2.9]
.

Proof.

Since
T
is a symmetric object of
\mathcal{C}
, this follows from Robalo’s comparison of Hovey’s category of symmetric spectra, which is the content of
[
Rob15
, Theorem 2.26]
.
∎

2.3.
Log motivic spectra

Let
S
be a quasi-compact and quasi-separated scheme. Recall that the Tate object
\Lambda(1)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)
is given by the splitting
\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1})\simeq\Lambda\oplus\Lambda(1)[2]
. By
[
BPØ23
, Proposition 3.2.7]
, the object
\Lambda(1)
is symmetric in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)
. We define the symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-category of log motivic spectra by

\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda):=\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{\Lambda(1)}^{\Sigma}\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda),

cf. Proposition
2.13
. For
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
, we let
X(n):=X\otimes\Lambda(1)^{\otimes n}
. An object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda)
is then a sequence
(X_{i},\sigma_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
where
X_{n}
are
({\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}},(\mathbf{P}^{\bullet},\mathbf{P}^{\bullet-1}))
-local

1
1

1

Recall that if
S
is finite dimensional and Noetherian this is the same as
(\mathrm{d\acute{e}t},{\overline{\square}})
-local by
[
BPØ23
,
Theorems 3.5.5 and 3.5.6]

objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
equipped with an action of the symmetric group
\Sigma_{n}
and
\sigma_{n}\colon X_{n}(1)\to X_{n+1}
are maps of complexes of presheaves such that:

i)

The composite

X_{n}(p)\to X_{n+1}(p-1)\to\ldots\to X_{n+p}

is
\Sigma_{n}\times\Sigma_{p}
-equivariant, where
\Sigma_{p}
acts on the left as the permutation isomorphism of the tensor structure of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)
and on the right via the embedding
\Sigma_{n}\times\Sigma_{p}\to\Sigma_{n+p}
.

ii)

The map
X_{n}\to{\underline{\operatorname{map}}}(\Lambda(1),X_{n+1})

adjoint to
\sigma_{n}
is an equivalence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)
.

If
R
is a
p
-complete ring with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion, we define
\Lambda(1)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(R,\Lambda)
in the same way as before. It is the image of
\Lambda(1)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(R,\Lambda)
under the strong monoidal functor
\mathrm{Comp_{!}}
of (
2.6.1
), so
\Lambda(1)
is also symmetric in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(R,\Lambda)
. We define the the symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-category

\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(R,\Lambda):=\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{\Lambda_{\rm F}(1)}^{\Sigma}\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(R,\Lambda),

whose objects are sequences
(X_{n},\sigma_{n})
, analogously to the case above.
The adjunction (
2.6.1
) induces canonically an adjunction

(2.13.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(R,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(R,\Lambda)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Comp}_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Comp}^{*}}

Definition 2.14
.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r^{L,\otimes}}
be a presentable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category. The category of graded objects of
\mathcal{C}
is the functor category
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{Z}^{\rm ds}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C})
. It is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, under the Day convolution product. A graded commutative monoid in
\mathcal{C}
is, by definition, an object of
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{Z}^{\rm ds}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}))
, where the symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}}_{\geq 0}
is given by the sum.

Remark 2.15
.

A graded commutative monoid
E_{*}
of
\mathcal{C}
is a sequence
\{E_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of objects of
\mathcal{C}
equipped with a unit map
\eta\colon\mathbbm{1}\to E_{0}
and a coherent commutative and associative multiplication. In particular, we obtain in the homotopy category
h\mathcal{C}
the following set of data. For any pair of integers
(i,j)
a multiplication
\mu_{i,j}\colon E_{i}\otimes E_{j}\to E_{i+j}
such that the diagrams commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i}\otimes E_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i}\otimes E_{j}\otimes E_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i}\otimes E_{j+k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i}\otimes E_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i+j}\otimes E_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i+j+k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{j}\otimes E_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i+j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{1,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes\mu_{j,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{i,j}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{i,j+k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{i,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{i,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{i+j,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{j,i}}

where
\gamma_{i,k}
is the symmetry isomorphism.

For
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}(S)
, let
R\Gamma(X,F)=\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{S},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))}(\Lambda(X),F)
. We write
\widetilde{R\Gamma}(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},F)
for
\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{S},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))}(\Lambda(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1})/\Lambda(X),F)
.
The following proposition is then analogous to
[
DM15
, Proposition 1.4.10]
:

Proposition 2.16
.

Let
E_{*}
be a graded commutative monoid in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,A)
) together with a section
c\colon\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1})\to E_{1}[2]
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
(resp. in
\operatorname{\mathbf{PSh}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
) such that for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R}
) and all
i
, the following composition is an equivalence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X,E_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},E_{i}\otimes\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},E_{i}\otimes E_{1}[2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{R\Gamma}(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},E_{i+1}[2])}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{i,1}}

Then there is
\mathbf{E}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda))
(resp.
\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\Lambda))
) such that for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{S}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R}
),

\displaystyle\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda)}(\Sigma^{\infty}(X),\Sigma^{m,n}\mathbf{E})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(X,E_{n}[m])

\displaystyle\text{(resp.\ }\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\Lambda)}(\Sigma^{\infty}(X),\Sigma^{m,n}\mathbf{E})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(X,E_{n}[m])\text{).}

Proof.

We prove it only for
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}
: the proof for
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}
proceeds
verbatim
. Consider the symmetric sequence
\mathbf{E}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,A)^{\Sigma}
given by setting
\mathbf{E}_{i}=E_{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, where each
E_{i}
is considered as
\Sigma_{i}
-module with trivial action. More concretely,
\mathbf{E}
is the image of
E_{*}
along the functor

(2.16.1)

\mathrm{Fun}^{L}(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\rm ds},\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda))\to\prod_{n}\mathrm{Fun}^{L}(B\Sigma_{n},\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda))=\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)^{\Sigma}

induced by the projection
B\Sigma_{\mathbb{N}}=\oplus_{n}B\Sigma_{n}\to\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\rm ds}
. Note that the functor (
2.16.1
) is symmetric monoidal, hence it induces a functor

(2.16.2)

\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Fun}^{L}(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\rm ds},\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)))\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)^{\Sigma}).

Since
E_{*}
is by assumption a graded commutative monoid, we obtain that
\mathbf{E}
is a commutative algebra object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,A)^{\Sigma}
.
For all
i
, the section
c
induces maps

(2.16.3)

E_{i}(1)\to E_{i}\otimes\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{S})[-2]\to E_{i}\otimes E_{1}\to E_{i+1},

in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)
, which build up to a map
A(1)\{-1\}\to\mathbf{E}
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,A)^{\Sigma}
, since
A(1)
is a symmetric object. By the universal property, we obtain a morphism of algebras
S_{A(1)}\to\mathbf{E}
, so that
\mathbf{E}\in\mathrm{Pre}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}^{\Sigma}_{A(1)}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda))
.

We are left to show that
\mathbf{E}
is indeed a symmetric spectrum: this amounts to checking that the induced map

E_{i}\to{\underline{\operatorname{map}}}(A(1),E_{i+1})

is an equivalence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)
for all
i
: by construction, for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}(S)
the map

\operatorname{map}(A(X),E_{i})\to\operatorname{map}(A(X)(1),E_{i+1})\simeq\operatorname{map}(A(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1})/A(X),E_{i+1}[2])

agrees with the map
R\Gamma(X,E_{i})\to\widetilde{R\Gamma}(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},E_{i+1}[2])
in the assumption, hence it is an equivalence.
Since the localization
\mathrm{Pre}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}^{\Sigma}_{A(1)}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda))\to\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda)
is symmetric monoidal, we conclude that
\mathbf{E}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda))
.
∎

Proposition 2.17
.

Let
E_{*}
and
E^{\prime}_{*}
be graded commutative monoids in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)
(resp. in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(R,\Lambda)
) with
c\colon\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1})\to E_{1}[2]
and
c^{\prime}\colon\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1})\to E_{1}^{\prime}[2]
that satisfy the hypotheses of Proposition
2.16
, and let
\mathbf{E},\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda)
(resp. in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(R,\Lambda)
) be the associated spectra. Let
\psi_{*}\colon E_{*}\to E^{\prime}_{*}
be a map of graded commutative monoids such that
\psi_{1}[2]\circ c=c^{\prime}
. Then there exists a map
\psi\colon\mathbf{E}\to\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda))
(resp. in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(R,\Lambda))
)
such that for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R}
), the map

R\Gamma(X,E_{n}[m])\simeq\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(X),\Sigma^{m,n}\mathbf{E})\xrightarrow{\psi_{X}}\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(X),\Sigma^{m,n}\mathbf{E}^{\prime})\simeq R\Gamma(X,E^{\prime}_{n}[m])

agrees with
\psi_{n}
.

Proof.

The morphism
\psi_{*}\colon E_{*}\to E_{*}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Fun}^{L}(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\rm ds},\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)))
induces a morphism

\psi\colon\mathbf{E}\to\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)^{\Sigma})
via the symmetric monoidal functor (
2.16.2
) induced by
B\Sigma_{\mathbb{N}}=\oplus_{n}B\Sigma_{n}\to\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\rm ds}
.
If it is a map in
\mathrm{Pre}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}^{\Sigma}_{\Lambda(1)}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda))
, then
\psi
is indeed a map in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda))
satisfying the desired property. By the compatibility with
c
, we have that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i}(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{i+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\prime}_{i}(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\prime}_{i+1},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}(1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i+1}}

commutes, where the horizontal maps come from (
2.16.3
). By the universal property, the map
\psi
is a map of
S_{\Lambda(1)}
-algebras. The proof for
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}
is analogous.
∎

Recall from
[
BPØ23
, Definition 7.1.3]
that a homotopy commutative monoid
\mathbf{E}
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda)
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(R,\Lambda)
) admits a
Chern orientation
, or simply that it is
oriented
if there is a class
c_{\infty}\in\pi_{0}\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathbf{P}^{\infty}),\mathbf{E}(1)[2])
whose restriction to
\mathbf{P}^{1}/{\rm pt}
is the map

\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathbf{P}^{1})\otimes\eta\colon\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathbf{P}^{1})\to\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathbf{P}^{1})\otimes\mathbf{E}\simeq E(1)[2].

Remark 2.18
.

Oriented spectra are particularly nice for the following reason (see
[
BLPØ23a
, Theorem 9.4]
): Let
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S}
: by
[
BPØ22b
, Lemma A.5.10]

|\partial X|
is a divisor with simple normal crossing. Let
{\underline{Z}}\subseteq{\underline{X}}
a smooth closed subscheme of relative codimension
d
with normal crossing with
|\partial X|
, so that
Z:=({\underline{Z}},\partial X_{|Z})\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S}
. Let
\partial{\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)
be the log structure on
{\mathbf{Bl}}_{\underline{Z}}({\underline{X}})
induced by the total transform of
|\partial X|
, so that also
{\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X):=({\mathbf{Bl}}_{\underline{Z}}({\underline{X}}),\partial{\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X))\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S}
. Then by
[
BPØ23
, Theorem 3.2.14]
for all oriented
\mathbf{E}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda)
there is a fiber sequence

(2.18.1)

\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z),\Sigma^{r-d,s-d}\mathbf{E})\to\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})\to\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}).

Moreover, if
\mathbf{E}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\Lambda)
is oriented, let
\mathrm{Comp}^{*}
be the right adjoint of (
2.13.1
): for all
Y\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{R}
and all
r,s
we have an equivalence

\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(R,\Lambda)}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Y),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathrm{Comp}^{*}(-))\simeq\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(R,\Lambda)}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Y_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r,s}(-)),

by adjunction. In particular,
\mathrm{Comp}^{*}
preserves oriented objects. This implies that for all
X
and
Z
as above, we have a fiber sequence

(2.18.2)

\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r-d,s-d}\mathbf{E})\to\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})\to\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}).

Remark 2.19
.

Let
X
be a smooth scheme over
S
and let
Z\subseteq X
be closed subscheme of pure codimension
d
such that
Z\to X\to S
is smooth. Let
X^{\prime}={\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)
and
Z^{\prime}=Z\times_{X}X^{\prime}
. Then by
[
BPØ22b
, Theorem 7.3.3]
, for all
\mathbf{E}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda)
there is a cartesian square, functorial in
(X,Z)
,

(2.19.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X^{\prime}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z^{\prime}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}).}

Let
X^{\prime\prime}
be the blow-up of
X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
in
Z\times 0
,
E
be the log structure on
X^{\prime\prime}
induced by the exceptional divisor and let
Z^{\prime\prime}=(Z\times 0)\times_{(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1})}X^{\prime\prime}
.
By
[
BPØ22b
, Theorem 7.4.2]
, there is a cartesian square, functorial in
(X,Z)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X\times(\mathbf{P}^{1},\infty)),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z\times 0),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X^{\prime\prime},E),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z^{\prime\prime}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}).}

By an argument completely analogous to
[
MVW06
, Corollary 15.13]
, the (homotopic) equivalence

1_{X}\times 0\sim 1_{X}\times 1\colon\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X)\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X\times(\mathbf{P}^{1},\infty))

gives a splitting of the map

\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z\times 0),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})\oplus\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X^{\prime\prime}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})\to\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z^{\prime\prime}),\Sigma^{r-2i,s-i}\mathbf{E}).

For all oriented
\mathbf{E}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(S,\Lambda)
, the map

\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z^{\prime\prime}),\Sigma^{r-2i,s-i}\mathbf{E})\to\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z^{\prime}),\Sigma^{r-2i,s-i}\mathbf{E})

is a split epimorphism, since it comes from the embedding
\mathbf{P}(N_{Z\subseteq X})\to\mathbf{P}(N_{Z\subseteq X}\oplus\mathcal{O})
: combining everything we get that the fiber sequence induced by (
2.19.1
) splits, giving an equivalence:

(2.19.2)

\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})\oplus(\oplus_{0<i<d}\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z),\Sigma^{r-2i,s-i}\mathbf{E}))\simeq\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X^{\prime}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}).

As before, via the adjunction (
2.13.1
) we deduce that for all
\mathbf{E}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\Lambda)
there is a cartesian square, functorial in
(X,Z)
:

(2.19.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda((X^{\prime})_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{i=1}^{d-1}\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda((Z^{\prime})_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})}

and for
\mathbf{E}
oriented we have an equivalence

(2.19.4)

\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(X_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E})\oplus(\oplus_{0<i<d}\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda(Z_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r-2i,s-i}\mathbf{E}))\simeq\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}\Lambda((X^{\prime})_{p}^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}).

2.4.
First examples: de Rham and crystalline motivic spectra

Let
R
be a
p
-complete ring. Consider the Hodge-completed derived log de Rham complex

\widehat{L\Omega}_{-/R}\colon\mathrm{PreLog}_{R}\to\widehat{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}}(R)

equipped with the Hodge filtration with graded pieces
(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{-/R})_{p}^{\wedge}
. It satisfies strict étale descent, so we can globalize it to formal
R
-schemes. Let
S
be a
p
-completed
R
-algebra and consider the strict étale sheaf

\widehat{L\Omega}_{-/R}\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{S}\to\mathcal{D}(R)\quad\mathfrak{X}\mapsto R\Gamma(\mathfrak{X},\widehat{L\Omega}_{-/R}).

Similarly, we consider the strict étale sheaves

(2.19.5)

L\Omega^{i}_{-/R}\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{S}\to\mathcal{D}(R)\quad\mathfrak{X}\mapsto R\Gamma(\mathfrak{X},(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{-/R})_{p}^{\wedge});

for
i\geq 0
. The exterior product of differential forms can be used to assemble (
2.19.5
) into a graded commutative monoid
\{L\Omega^{i}_{-/R}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R},\mathcal{D}(R))
. Similarly, the cdga structure of the log de Rham complex and the multiplicativity of the Hodge filtration induce a structure of a (constant) graded commutative monoid on
\{\widehat{L\Omega}_{-/R}\}_{i}
(see e.g.
[
Bha12a
, 4.1]
).

Let now
R
be a perfectoid or
R=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
: we can consider the
p
-completed derived log de Rham complex equipped with the conjugate filtration:

L\Omega_{-/R}\colon\mathrm{PreLog}_{R}\to\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}(R).

With an argument completely analogous to
[
BMS19
, Example 5.12]
, it is a log quasisyntomic sheaf.

Proposition 2.20
.

(1)

There are oriented
\mathbf{E}^{\rm Hdg},\mathbf{E}^{\rm\widehat{dR}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,R))
such that for all
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{S}
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{p,q}\mathbf{E}^{\rm Hdg})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(\mathfrak{X},L\Omega^{q}_{-/R})[p],

\displaystyle\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{p,q}\mathbf{E}^{\rm\widehat{dR}})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(\mathfrak{X},\widehat{L\Omega}_{-/R})[p].

(2)

Let
R
be perfectoid or
R=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}_{R}
. Then there is an oriented homotopy commutative monoid
\mathbf{E}^{\rm dR}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,R))
such that

\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{p,q}\mathbf{E}^{\rm{dR}})\simeq R\Gamma(\mathfrak{X},{L\Omega}_{-/R})[p]

Proof.

We begin by showing that
(L\Omega^{i}_{-/R})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is in fact a graded commutative monoid in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,R)
.
By strict étale descent it is enough to show that for any pre-log
R
-algebra
(A,M)
, the projection induces an equivalence

(2.20.1)

R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(A,\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R})\simeq R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{A},\mathbb{L}_{(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{(A,M)},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{(A,M)})/R}).

By left Kan extension from the case of finite free pre-log algebras, this follows from
[
BLPØ23a
, Proposition 8.3]
.
Next, for
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{S}
and
\mathcal{E}\to\mathfrak{X}
a vector bundle of rank
r+1
, consider the map induced by the first Chern class:

(2.20.2)

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}R\Gamma(\mathfrak{X},L\Omega^{j-i}_{\mathfrak{X}/R})[-2i]\to R\Gamma(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}),L\Omega^{j}_{\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}))/R}).

By choosing a trivializing cover and by an argument completely analogous to
[
BL22
, B.8]
we have an equivalence

R\Gamma(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{\mathfrak{X}},L\Omega^{j}_{\mathbf{P}^{n}_{\mathfrak{X}}/R})\simeq R\Gamma(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{R},\Omega^{j}_{\mathbf{P}^{n}_{R}/R})\otimes_{R}^{L}R\Gamma(\mathfrak{X},L\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/R})

compatible with the Chern classes, so the classical computation of the Hodge cohomology of projective spaces
[
Sta16
,
Tag 0FMI
]
implies that the map (
2.20.2
) is an equivalence. We can then apply Proposition
2.16
to obtain an oriented spectrum
\mathbf{E}^{\rm Hdg}
.

We now pass to derived de Rham cohomology. By passing to the graded pieces of the filtration, we see from the above computation that
\widehat{L\Omega}_{-/R}
is
(\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})
-invariant, so
\widehat{L\Omega}_{-/R}
is a (constant) commutative monoid in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,R)
. The first Chern class again induces a map

(2.20.3)

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}R\Gamma(\mathfrak{X},\widehat{L\Omega}_{-/R})[-2i]\to R\Gamma(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}),\widehat{L\Omega}_{-/R})

which is an equivalence by passing to the graded pieces and noting that (
2.20.2
) is an equivalence. The spectrum
\mathbf{E}^{\rm\widehat{dR}}
is then assembled using again Proposition
2.16
. This proves
(1)
.

Let now
R
be a perfectoid or
R=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. By induction on
n
and (
2.20.1
), we have similarly to
[
BMS19
, Example 5.12]
that for all
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{R}
, and all
m
,

R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(A,\operatorname{Fil}_{m}^{\rm conj}L\Omega_{(A,M)/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})\simeq R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{A},\operatorname{Fil}_{m}^{\rm conj}L\Omega_{\mathbf{P}^{n}_{(A,M)},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{(A,M)}}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}),

and since
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{R}
, by Lemma
4.16
, we have that
\operatorname{Fil}_{m}^{\rm conj}L\Omega_{(A,M)/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
takes value in
\mathcal{D}^{\geq-1}
for all
n
, as each
\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}
has
p
-completed Tor amplitude
[-1,0]
: this implies that cohomology commutes with filtered colimits so we conclude that

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(A,L\Omega_{(A,M)/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{m}R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(A,\operatorname{Fil}_{m}^{\rm conj}L\Omega_{(A,M)/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{m}R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{A},\operatorname{Fil}_{m}^{\rm conj}L\Omega_{\mathbf{P}^{n}_{(A,M)},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{(A,M)}}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{A},L\Omega_{\mathbf{P}^{n}_{(A,M)},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{(A,M)}}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})

Again
L\Omega_{/R}
is a (constant) commutative monoid and the first Chern class induces an equivalence analogous to (
2.20.3
) again by considering
\operatorname{Fil}_{m}^{\rm conj}
and passing to the filtered colimit. This proves
(2)

∎

Let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p
. For
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{k}
, and
m,n\geq 0
, let
W_{m}\Omega^{n}_{X/k}
be the log de Rham–Witt sheaf of
[
Mat17
]
and let
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/k}
the
m
-truncated log de Rham–Witt complex.

Proposition 2.21
.

There are oriented

\mathbf{E}^{m,\rm dRW},\mathbf{E}^{m,\rm crys},\mathbf{E}^{\rm crys}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(k,W(k)))

such that for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{k}
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(X),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{m,\rm dRW})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(X,W_{m}\Omega^{s}_{X/k})[r]

\displaystyle\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(X),\Sigma^{p,q}\mathbf{E}^{m,\rm crys})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(X/W_{m}(k))[p]

\displaystyle\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(X),\Sigma^{p,q}\mathbf{E}^{\rm crys})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(X/W(k))[p]

Proof.

Recall from
[
Mer22
, Theorem 1.3]
that for all
m
there is
W_{m}\Omega^{s}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(k,W(k))
representing the cohomology of
W_{m}\Omega^{s}{\_/k}
. By considering the graded commutative monoid
\{W_{m}\Omega^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, the crystalline Chern class of
[
Gro85
]
induces a section
c_{m}\colon\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1})\to W_{m}\Omega^{1}[2]
that induces, for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{k}
, an equivalence (see
[
Mer22
, (4.5.1)]
)

R\Gamma(X,W_{m}\Omega^{i})\simeq\widetilde{R\Gamma}(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},W_{m}\Omega^{i+1})[2]

By applying Proposition
2.16
, we get oriented ring spectra
\mathbf{E}^{m,\rm dRW}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(k,W(k)))
.
By considering the
m
-truncated de Rham–Witt complex, again by
[
Mer22
, Theorem 1.3]
, we get again a constant graded commutative monoid
\{W_{m}\Omega\}
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(k,W(k))
producing oriented ring spectra
\mathbf{E}^{m,\rm crys}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(k,W(k)))
with, for all
m_{1}\geq m_{2}
, maps of oriented motivic spectra
\mathbf{E}^{m_{1},\rm crys}\to\mathbf{E}^{m_{2},\rm crys}

in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(k,W(k))
by Proposition
2.17
. Then
\mathbf{E}^{\rm crys}:=\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{m}\mathbf{E}^{m,\rm crys}
.
∎

If
F\colon W(k)\to W(k)
is the Frobenius on
W(k)
, we let
F^{*}\mathbf{E}^{\rm crys}
be the oriented ring spectrum with
W(k)
-module structure twisted by
F
.

Remark 2.22
.

As proven in
[
Mer22
]
,
\mathbf{E}^{m,\rm dRW}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\rm crys}
are ring spectra in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DM}}(k,W(k))
. This will suggest, via the crystalline comparison, that the prismatic and syntomic cohomology are presheaves with logarithmic transfers (in an appropriate sense): this will be investigated in future work.

3.
Prismatic and syntomic realizations

We recall from
[
BLPØ23b
, Definition 4.9]
(which is simply a translation to the log setting of
[
BMS19
, Definition 4.10(3)]
) the following definition:

Definition 3.1
.

A pre-log ring
(A,M)
is called
log quasisyntomic
if
A
is
p
-complete with bounded
p^{\infty}
torsion,
p
-completely flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and satisfies the property that
\mathbb{L}_{(A,M_{A})/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
has
p
-complete Tor amplitude in degree
[-1,0]
.
We denote by
\operatorname{lQSyn}
the category of pre-log rings that are log quasisyntomic, and for any ring
R
, we let
\operatorname{lQSyn}_{R}
denote the category of pre-log
R
-algebras which are log quasisyntomic.
Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a quasi-coherent bounded
p
-adic formal log scheme. Then
\mathfrak{X}
is
log quasisyntomic
if strict étale locally it is isomorphic to
\operatorname{Spf}(A,M)^{a}
with
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}
. We will denote
\operatorname{\mathrm{FlQSyn}}
the category of bounded
p
-adic log quasisyntomic formal log schemes.

We also recall the following notion:

Definition 3.2
.

An integral pre-log ring
(S,Q)
is called log quasiregular
semiperfectoid if it is log quasisyntomic, there exists a map
R\to S
with
R
perfectoid, and
S/pS
and
Q
are semiperfect (in the sense of Definition
4.1
).

Remark 3.3
.

If
\operatorname{Spf}(A,M)\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R}
, then
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{R}
by construction. Hence any object of
\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{lQSyn}_{R},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
with quasisyntomic descent naturally defines an object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{R},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
.

Recall from
[
BLPØ23b
, 7.7]
that, analogously to
[
BMS19
, §7]
, the Nygaard-complete absolute log prismatic cohomology

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}:=R\Gamma_{\rm lqsyn}(-,\pi_{0}\operatorname{TP}(-)_{p}^{\wedge})
equipped with the Nygaard filtration define functors

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{lQSyn},\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq i}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\in\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{lQSyn},\widehat{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})).

These complexes commute with (homotopy) limits and satisfy quasisyntomic descent (this is a consequence of
[
BLPØ23b
, Theorem 2.9]
and the construction).
Define the Breuil–Kisin twists

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{1\}

\displaystyle:=R\Gamma_{\rm lqsyn}(-,\pi_{2}\operatorname{TP}(-)_{p}^{\wedge})[-2]

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{-1\}

\displaystyle:=R\Gamma_{\rm lqsyn}(-,\pi_{-2}\operatorname{TP}(-)_{p}^{\wedge})[2]

with the Nygaard filtrations given by unfolding the double-speed Postnikov filtration.
In analogy with
[
BMS19
, Theorem 1.12 (3)]
, we get the twisted prismatic cohomology
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{i\}
by taking tensor powers in
\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{lQSyn},\widehat{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
, with the induced filtration.

As observed in
[
BMS19
, Lemma 7.14]
, the object
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}\{1\}
is not invertible as an
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}
-module, but it is invertible when considered a module over the
filtered ring

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}
(the proof for log rings works verbatim). In particular, in the filtered category
\widehat{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, we have an equivalence

\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{i\}\otimes\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{j\}\to\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{i+j\}

of filtered objects for all
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
, where the tensor product is taken in
\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{lQSyn},\widehat{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}}({\mathbb{Z}_{p}}))
. By definition of the convolution product, we have

\operatorname{Fil}^{m}(\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{i\}\otimes\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{j\})\simeq\bigoplus_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m}\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq m_{1}}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{i\}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq m_{2}}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{j\}.

This gives associative and commutative multiplication maps in
\mathrm{Fun}(\operatorname{lQSyn},\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))

(3.3.1)

\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq m}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{i\}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq n}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{j\}\to\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq m+n}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-}\{i+j\}

for all
i,j,m,n
.

For every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we denote by
R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}
(resp.
\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}
) the object defined in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlQSyn}},\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
(resp. in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlQSyn}},\widehat{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
) by descent.

Remark 3.4
.

Since the filtrations obtained by unfolding from the double-speed Postnikov filtration of (logarithmic)
\operatorname{TC}^{-}
and
\operatorname{TP}
are functorial and multiplicative, we have that

R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm lqsyn}(-,\pi_{2i}\operatorname{TP}(-)_{p}^{\wedge})[-2i]

and

\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq i}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm lqsyn}(-,\pi_{2i}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(-)_{p}^{\wedge})[-2i].

The multiplication maps in (
3.3.1
) agree with the one induced by the
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-structure of
\operatorname{TP}
and
\operatorname{TC}^{-}
, which also satisfy the identity axiom: this implies that the objects
\{R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
and
\{\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq i}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
are graded commutative monoids in their respective categories.

Remark 3.5
.

The filtration
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)
is complete and exhaustive (in fact, constant in negative degrees).
Indeed, since the category
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlQSyn}},\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}))
is generated by
\mathbb{Z}(U)
with
U=\operatorname{Spf}(A,M)
, it is enough to check that

\displaystyle\operatorname{map}(\mathbb{Z}(U),\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq n}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R))

\displaystyle=\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}\operatorname{map}(\mathbb{Z}(U),\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq n}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R))

\displaystyle=\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq n}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)/R}=0

and, since the colimit is filtered and
\mathbb{Z}(U)
is compact, for all
m\geq 0
the map

\operatorname{map}(\mathbb{Z}(U),\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq-m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-))\to\operatorname{map}(\mathbb{Z}(U),\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq-n}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-))

agrees with the map

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}\to\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq-n}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)},

which is an equivalence (this can be checked
\operatorname{lQSyn}
-locally, reducing to the case of
\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}
, where it is obvious since the Postnikov filtration is exhaustive, so
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)
is eventually constant.

Remark 3.6
.

If
R
is perfectoid ring, let
\Lambda:={\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\cong A_{\rm inf}(R)
. By restriction and descent, we get an object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlQSyn}}_{R},\mathcal{D}(\Lambda))
(resp. in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Shv}}_{{\operatorname{s\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{\mathrm{FlQSyn}}_{R},\widehat{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}}(\Lambda))
) that we denote
R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R)
(resp. by
\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R)
) In this case, we have that the graded pieces
\operatorname{gr}_{N}^{i}(R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}))
carry themselves a finite
secondary
