filtration with graded pieces given by

\operatorname{map}(\mathbb{Z}(U),\operatorname{gr}^{j}\operatorname{gr}_{N}^{i}(R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R)))=(L\Omega^{j}_{(A,M)/R})_{p}^{\wedge}[-j]

Remark 3.7
.

The perfectoid base is useful for the following reason: the Breuil-Kisin twist of
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}
is given as in
[
BMS19
, §6.2]
by
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\{1\}:=\pi_{2}\operatorname{TP}(R;\mathbb{Z}_{p})\in\mathcal{D}({\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R})
, defined in terms of the (non-logarithmic) prismatic cohomology. Since
R
is perfectoid, it is a free module of rank
1
over
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}
generated by the element
\xi\in A_{\inf}(R)
. In this case, the
i
-th twisted prismatic cohomology satisfies

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-/R}\{i\}\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-/R}\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}^{L}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\{1\}^{\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}i},

Similarly, the twisted Nygaard filtration satisfies

\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq i}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-/R}\{i\}\simeq\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq i}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-/R}\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}^{L}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\{1\}^{\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}i}.

Remark 3.8
.

We can interpret the filtered object
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-/R}\{i\}
as the convolution product of the filtered objects
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-/R}
and
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\{1\}^{\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}i}
, where the latter is equipped with the trivial filtration. With this convention, the object
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R/R}\{i\}
comes equipped with its Nygaard filtration as in
[
BMS19
, §6.2]
, given by
\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq n}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R/R}\{i\}=\xi^{n}A_{\inf}(R)u^{i}
, where
u
is a generator of
\pi_{2}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(R;\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. This agrees with the filtration given by the convolution product on

({\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-/R}\{1\})^{\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}}}i}.

By quasisyntomic descent, this recovers the filtration on the absolute prismatic cohomology.

We recall the non-Nygaard complete version:

Construction 3.9
.

Let
R
be a perfectoid ring and let
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{-/R}
be the functor obtained by left Kan extension from
p
-completion of derived
2
2

2

Since
R
has trivial log structure, here derived log smooth agrees with log smooth by
[
BLPØ23a
, Proposition 4.6]
, as every map from a ring with trivial log structure is integral.
log smooth pre-log
R
-algebras. Then as in
[
BMS19
, Construction 7.12]
,
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{-/R}/\xi\simeq L\Omega_{-/R}
, where
L\Omega_{-/R}
is the
p
-completed derived de Rham cohomology. As observed in
[
BMS19
, Construction 7.12]
, this depends on the choice of a perfectoid base.
By the comparison with
L\Omega
, we deduce that
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}
is a quasisyntomic sheaf on
\operatorname{lQSyn}_{R}
with values in
\mathcal{D}(A_{\inf}(R))
and that it takes discrete values on
\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}_{R}
: thus, it can be globalized as before and we consider the quasisyntomic sheaves
R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)
and the Breuil–Kisin twists given by tensoring with
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\{1\}
(note that
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}^{\rm nc}
by construction). As observed in the proof of
[
BS22
, Theorem 13.1]
, it comes equipped with the Nygaard filtration
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(S/R)\{i\}
again by left Kan extension, and
R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(S/R)\{i\}
is the completion of
R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(S/R)\{i\}
with respect to this filtration: this follows from the comparison of the graded pieces.

We expect this to compare with the log prismatic cohomology of
[
Kos22
]
and
[
KY23
]
, and moreover that an absolute version of this non-Nygaard complete log prismatic cohomology similar to the one of
[
BL22
]
also exists and compares to both: we leave this for future work.

Proposition 3.10
.

Assume that
R
is a perfectoid ring. Let
S\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{R}
. For all
i,m\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n\geq 1
, we have equivalences:

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(S/R)\{i\}

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(((\mathbf{P}^{n}_{R},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{R})\otimes_{R}S)/R)\{i\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(S/R)\{i\}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(((\mathbf{P}^{n}_{R},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{R})\otimes_{R}S)/R)\{i\}

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(S/R)\{i\}

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(((\mathbf{P}^{n}_{R},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{R})\otimes_{R}S)/R)\{i\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(S/R)\{i\}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(((\mathbf{P}^{n}_{R},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{R})\otimes_{R}S)/R)\{i\}.

In particular, for all
S\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{R}
, the strict étale sheaves

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R)\{i\},\quad\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R)\{i\},

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)\{i\},\quad\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)\{i\}

are
(\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})
-invariant, so they define objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)
.

Proof.

Via the trivialization
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\{1\}\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}
(which can be chosen since
R
is perfectoid) we reduce to the case where
i=0
. Let us prove the completed version first.
Since the filtration is complete and exhaustive, it is enough to prove it for
\operatorname{gr}_{N}^{i}(R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R))
. Since the secondary filtration they carry by Remark
3.6
is finite, it is enough to check it on the graded pieces, i.e. it is enough to check that
L\Omega^{j}_{-/R}
is
(\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})
-local: this is done in Proposition
2.20
.

For the non-complete version, we deduce the result for
R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)
by checking it modulo
\xi
from the analogous result for
p
-completed de Rham cohomology
2.20
. Finally, we deduce the statement for the filtration from the sequence

\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)\to\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)\to\operatorname{gr}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R)

plus the fact that
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)\simeq R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)
for
m\leq 0
.
∎

Corollary 3.11
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlQSyn}}
. For all
i,m\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n\geq 1
, we have equivalences:

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{i\}

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}((\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})\times\mathfrak{X})\{i\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{i\}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}((\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})\times\mathfrak{X})\{i\}.

In particular, for all
S\in\operatorname{lQSyn}
, the strict étale sheaves

R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\},\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{S}^{\rm op}\to\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

are
(\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})
-invariant, so they define objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Proof.

By descent, we can assume
\mathfrak{X}=\operatorname{Spf}(S)
with
S\in\operatorname{lQSyn}
. Let
S\to S^{\prime}
be a quasisyntomic cover in
\operatorname{lQSyn}
with
S^{\prime}\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}
and let
(S^{\prime})^{\bullet}
be its Čech nerve. Then there is a perfectoid ring
R
with a map
R\to S^{\prime}
so
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S^{\prime}}\{i\}\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S^{\prime}/R}\{i\}
and
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S}\{i\}\simeq\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S/R}\{i\}
. In particular, the projections
\mathbf{P}^{n}_{S}\to S
and
\mathbf{P}^{n}_{(S^{\prime})^{\bullet}}\to(S^{\prime})^{\bullet}
induce an equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(S)\{i\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}((S^{\prime})^{m}/R)\{i\}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{S},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{S})\{i\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}((\mathbf{P}^{n}_{(S^{\prime})^{m}},\mathbf{P}^{n-1}_{(S^{\prime})^{m}})/R)\{i\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{m\in\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
. Since the vertical maps on the right-hand side are equivalences by Proposition
3.10
, we deduce that the map on the left-hand side is an equivalence too. The result for
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}
follows in the same way.
∎

3.1.
The first Chern class

In order to apply Proposition
2.16
, we want to exploit the Chern classes defined in
[
BL22
]
.

We consider the non-Nygaard-complete absolute prismatic cohomology of (non-log) quasisyntomic bounded
p
-adic formal schemes
R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(-)\{i\}
of
[
BL22
]
with its Nygaard filtration
\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}_{N}R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(-)\{i\}
. For
{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}
a (non-log) bounded
p
-adic formal scheme, the filtered object
(R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}),\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}))
of
[
BMS19
]
coincides with the completion of the filtered object
(R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}),\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}_{N}R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}))
functorially in
{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}
(see
[
BL22
, Theorem 5.6.2]
and
[
BS22
, Theorem 13.1]
for the case over a perfectoid ring and then use quasisyntomic descent).
Recall that by
[
BL22
, Notation 7.5.3]
for all (non-log) bounded
p
-adic formal schemes
{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}
there is a Chern class map

c_{1}^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\colon R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}},\mathbf{G}_{m})[-1]\to\operatorname{Fil}^{1}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})\{1\},

Let
{\underline{\mathcal{E}}}\to{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}
be a vector bundle and let
\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})
be the associated projective bundle. Since
H^{0}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}},\mathbf{G}_{m})=0
and
H^{1}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}},\mathbf{G}_{m})=\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})\cong\mathbb{Z}
, we have a Chern class given by:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}\cong\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\leq 0}R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}),\mathbf{G}_{m})[1]\to R\Gamma_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}}),\mathbf{G}_{m})[1]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{c_{1}^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[2]}\operatorname{Fil}^{1}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}}))\{1\})[2],

and by composing with
\operatorname{Fil}^{1}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(-)\{1\}[2]\to R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(-)\{1\}[2]
we get

c_{1}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\colon\mathbb{Z}\cong\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})\to R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}}))\{1\}[2].

More generally, for all
i\geq 0
we get

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Fil}^{1}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}}))\{1\}[2]^{\otimes i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Fil}^{i}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}}))\{i\}[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})^{\otimes i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}}))\{1\}[2]^{\otimes i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}}))\{i\})[2i]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}

If
{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}
is a bounded
p
-adic formal
R
-scheme (with trivial log structure), we obtain by
[
BS22
, Theorem 13.1]

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})\{i\}

\displaystyle\to\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})\{i\}/\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq n}R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})\{i\}\simeq R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})\{i\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet}R\Gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})\{i\}

\displaystyle\to\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{n}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})\{i\}/\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet+n}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}/R)\{i\}\simeq\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})\{i\}

induced by completions. This gives Chern classes

\displaystyle(c_{1}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}})^{i}\colon\mathbb{Z}\cong\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq i}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}}))\{i\}[2i]

\displaystyle(c_{1}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}})^{i}\colon\mathbb{Z}\cong\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})

\displaystyle\to R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}}))\{i\}[2i].

Construction 3.12
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlQSyn}}
and
{\underline{\mathcal{E}}}\to{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}
a vector bundle, we let
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})
be the log schemes with the pullback log structure from
\mathfrak{X}
(see e.g.
[
BPØ22b
, Definition 7.1.2]
). Recall by
[
BS22
, Theorem 13.1]
that For all
i,m\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have maps

(3.12.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\mathfrak{X}})\{m-i\}[-2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\mathfrak{X}})\{m-i\}[-2i]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{L}\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\mathfrak{X}})\{m-i\}[-2i]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{L}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})/R)\{i\}[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})))\{m-i\}[-2i]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{L}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R)\{i\}[2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}))\{m\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id\otimes(c_{1}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}})^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(*)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

which is functorial in
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathcal{E}
. Here
(*)
is induced by the pull-back along the maps
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}})
. Similarly, we obtain a map

(3.12.2)

\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq m-i}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\mathfrak{X}})\{n-i\}[-2i]\to\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R)\{n\}.

The maps of (
3.12.2
) assemble to a map

(3.12.3)

\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet-i}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{n-i\}[-2i]\to\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}))\{n\}.

in
\widehat{\mathcal{DF}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for every
n,i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

In a completely analogous way, for
R
perfectoid using that
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}/R}\simeq{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}/R}
by
[
BS22
, Theorem 13.1]
we obtain a map

\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet-i}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(\mathfrak{X})\{n-i\}[-2i]\to\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}))\{n\}.

in
\mathcal{DF}(A_{\inf}(R))
for every
n,i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The following result is now analogous to
[
BL22
, Lemma 9.1.4]
.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
R
be a perfectoid ring and let
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlQSyn}}_{R}
. Let
\mathcal{E}\to\mathfrak{X}
be a vector bundle of rank
r+1
equipped with the induced log structure as above. The maps defined in (
3.12.1
) and (
3.12.2
) give equivalences for all
m,n
.

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m-i}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/R)\{n-i\}[-2i]\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R)\{n\}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/R)\{n-i\}[-2i]\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R)\{n\}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m-i}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(\mathfrak{X}/R)\{n-i\}[-2i]\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R)\{n\}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(\mathfrak{X}/R)\{n-i\}[-2i]\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R)\{n\}

Proof.

We follow the pattern of
[
BL22
, Lemma 9.1.4]
: we first prove the completed version.
Notice that the second equivalence is a special case of the first with
m\leq 0
.
As in (
3.12.3
), for fixed
n
we can build a map

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet-i}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/R)\{n-i\}[-2i]\rightarrow\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R)\{n\}

in
\widehat{\mathcal{DF}}({\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R})
. Since the filtration is complete it is enough to check that the induced map

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}\operatorname{gr}_{N}^{m-i}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/R)\{n-i\}[-2i]\xrightarrow{}\operatorname{gr}_{N}^{m}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R)\{n\}

on graded pieces is an equivalence by
[
BMS19
, Lemma 5.2 (1)]
. Let
I
be the kernel of the map
\theta\colon{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\to R
, generated by
\xi
. By
[
BMS19
, Remark 6.6]
, there is an equivalence of
R
-modules

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\{i\}\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}}^{L}R\simeq(I/I^{2})^{\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R}i}.

Since the structure of
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}
-module on
\operatorname{gr}^{m}_{N}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/R)
induces an
R
-module structure by
[
BMS19
, Proposition 7.8]
, we have that

\operatorname{gr}^{m}_{N}(R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/R)\{i\})\simeq\operatorname{gr}^{m}_{N}(R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/R))\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}}^{L}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\{i\}\simeq\operatorname{gr}^{m}_{N}(R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/R))\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R}^{L}(I/I^{2})^{\otimes i}.

This implies that the finite filtration on
\operatorname{gr}^{i}_{N}(R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/R))
induces a map

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}R\Gamma(\mathfrak{X},(\bigwedge^{j-i}\mathbb{L}_{\mathfrak{X}/R})_{p}^{\wedge}\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R}^{L}(I/I^{2})^{\otimes n-i})[-2i]\to R\Gamma(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}),\bigwedge^{j}\mathbb{L}_{\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R})_{p}^{\wedge}\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R}^{L}(I/I^{2})^{\otimes n}.

The map on
I/I^{2}
is just the multiplication. Since they are free
R
-modules of rank
1
, they can be coherently trivialized, which leaves us with the map

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}R\Gamma(\mathfrak{X},\bigwedge^{j-i}\mathbb{L}_{\mathfrak{X}/R})[-2i]\to R\Gamma(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}),\bigwedge^{j}\mathbb{L}_{\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R}).

By construction (see
[
BL22
, Theorem 7.6.2]
), this map agrees with the powers of the first Chern class
c_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{O}(1))
, which is an equivalence (see (
2.20.2
)).

For the non-completed version, in the same way as before we see that the twists trivialize modulo
\xi
and by construction as before, the Chern classes also agree with the de Rham chern class modulo
\xi
(see again
[
BL22
, Theorem 7.6.2]
): this gives the equivalence

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(\mathfrak{X}/R)\{n-i\}/\xi[-2i]

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}R\Gamma({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}},L\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/R})\{n-i\}\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma(\mathbf{P}({\underline{\mathcal{E}}}),L\Omega_{\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/R)\{n\}/\xi

which gives the fourth equation by
\xi
-completeness. Finally, again use the sequence

\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)\{i\}\to\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)\{i\}\to\operatorname{gr}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R)\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R}^{L}(I/I^{2})^{\otimes i}

plus the fact that
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)\simeq R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)
for
m\leq 0
to deduce the third equivalence.

∎

As before, we deduce the result in the absolute case:

Corollary 3.14
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlQSyn}}
and let
\mathcal{E}\to\mathfrak{X}
be a vector bundle of rank
r+1
equipped with the induced log structure as above. Then, for all
m
, the maps defined in (
3.12.1
) give equivalences

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m-i}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{n-i\}[-2i]\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}))\{n\}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{n-i\}[-2i]\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}))\{n\}

Proof.

As in the proof of Corollary
3.11
, we assume
\mathfrak{X}=\operatorname{Spf}(S)
with
S\in\operatorname{lQSyn}
and we let
S\to S^{\prime}
be a quasisyntomic cover in
\operatorname{lQSyn}
with
S^{\prime}\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}
,
(S^{\prime})^{\bullet}
the Čech nerve, and we fix
R\to S^{\prime}
with
R
perfectoid. We have an equivalence

\hbox to417.53pt{\vbox to87.88pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 213.00134pt\lower-43.93875pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-21.60536pt}{1.89436pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
. The result follows from Lemma
3.13
.
∎

We are now ready to prove our first main result.

Theorem 3.15
.

Let
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}
. There are oriented ring spectra
\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
such that for all
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{S}
we have

\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}})\simeq R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{s\}[r]

and

\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}})\simeq\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq s}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{s\}[r].

If
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}_{R}
for
R
perfectoid,
\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
live in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,A_{\inf}(R))
, and there are oriented ring spectra
\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,A_{\inf}(R)))
, together with equivalences

(3.15.1)

\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\otimes_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R},\theta}R\simeq\mathbf{E}^{\rm\widehat{dR}}\qquad\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}\otimes_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R},\theta}R\simeq\mathbf{E}^{\rm dR}

of oriented ring spectra in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,R))
, where
\theta\colon A_{\rm inf}(R)\to R
is Fontaine’s period map.

Proof.

Let
\{E_{i}\}
be either the collection
\{R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\{i\}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
or the collection
\{\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq i}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\{i\}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
.
By Corollary
3.11
and Remark
3.4
,
E_{*}
is a graded commutative monoid in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,\Lambda)
. Moreover, Proposition
3.10
gives equivalences

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\operatorname{Spf}(S)})\{1\}[2]\simeq\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S)}(\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1}),E_{1}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}[2])

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\operatorname{Spf}(S)})\{1\}[2]\simeq\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S)}(\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1}),E_{1}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}[2]),

from which we get a section
\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1})\to E_{1}[2]
. By Corollary
3.14
, the composition as constructed in Proposition
2.16
is an equivalence. So we can apply Proposition
2.16
and get the ring spectra
\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
. They are oriented by Lemma
3.13
.

If
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}_{R}
with
R
perfectoid, the same construction using Proposition
3.10
and Lemma
3.13
gives
\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}
. Moreover, we have equivalences of graded commutative monoids:

\displaystyle(R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R)\{i\}\otimes_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}}R)_{i}

\displaystyle\simeq(R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/R)\otimes_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}}(I/I^{2})^{\otimes i})_{i}

\displaystyle(R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)\{i\}\otimes_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}}R)_{i}

\displaystyle\simeq(R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(-/R)\otimes_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}}(I/I^{2})^{\otimes i})_{i}

similarly to Lemma
3.13
. Here
I
is the kernel of Fontaine’s period map
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{R}\to R
, generated by
\xi
. In particular, as before, the twists can be canonically trivialized and the natural equivalence

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-/R}/\xi\simeq\widehat{L\Omega_{-/R}}

of
[
BLPØ23b
, §7.2]
and the equivalence

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{-/R}/\xi\simeq L\Omega_{-/R}

obtained by left Kan extension, are indeed equivalences of graded commutative monoids in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(S,R)
by descent from
\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}
(analogously to
[
BMS19
, Proposition 7.9]
), which by Proposition
2.17
lift to the desired equivalences.
∎

3.2.
Syntomic cohomology

Recall that the canonical and Frobenius maps

\mathrm{can}\{n\},\varphi\{n\}\colon\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq n}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{n\}\to R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{n\}

arise via unfolding of the maps of graded commutative algebras

\pi_{2*}(\mathrm{can}),\pi_{2*}(\varphi)\colon\pi_{2*}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\pi_{2*}\operatorname{TP}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

These are functorial in
S\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}
(since they are functorial in
\mathrm{Alg}_{E_{1}}
, see e.g.
[
NS18
, §III]
), so in particular they induce maps of graded commutative monoids

\{\mathrm{can}\{i\}\},\{\varphi\{i\}\}\colon\{\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq i}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}\}_{i}\to\{R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}\}_{i}

by unfolding.
Let
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}
and
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{S}
, and let

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(s)):=\mathrm{fib}(\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq s}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{s\}[r]\xrightarrow{\varphi\{s\}-\mathrm{can}\{s\}}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{s\}[r])

for
s\in\mathbb{Z}
.
If
\mathfrak{X}
has trivial log structure, then it agrees with the syntomic cohomology of formal schemes of
[
BL22
, Construction 7.4.1]
(therein denoted
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{syn}}
: we keep the notation for schemes and formal schemes separate). Observe that the same proof as in
[
AMMN22
, Theorem 5.1]
implies that
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}
is left Kan extended from
p
-complete polynomial algebras, so in case
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}_{R}
for
R
perfectoid, it agrees with the same fiber taken in the non-Nygaard completed setting.

Theorem 3.16
.

Let
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}
. Then there is an oriented ring spectrum
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
such that for all
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{S}
we have

\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\Lambda)}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}})\simeq R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(\mathfrak{X})\{s\}[r].

Proof.

Let
c\colon\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1})\to E_{1}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}[2]
and
c^{\prime}\colon\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1})\to E_{1}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}[2]
be the maps constructed in the proof of Theorem
3.15
. By construction, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1})[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}[2]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces can\{1\}}

commutes. By Proposition
2.17
, there is a map of ring spectra
\mathrm{can}\colon\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\to\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\Lambda)
. Moreover, the two compositions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\rm et}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\operatorname{Spf}(S)},\mathbf{G}_{m})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Fil}^{1}_{N}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\operatorname{Spf}(S)})\{1\}[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Fil}^{1}_{N}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\operatorname{Spf}(S)})\{1\}[2]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{1}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces can\{1\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi\{1\}}

agree, since
c_{1}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
factors through the equalizer of
\mathrm{can}\{1\}
and
\varphi\{1\}
(see
[
BL22
, Notation 7.5.3]
). This implies that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda(\mathbf{P}^{1})[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}[2],}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

commutes.
By Proposition
2.17
, there is a map
\varphi\colon\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\to\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
of oriented ring spectra in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
.

Let
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}}
denote the equalizer in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})
of
\varphi-\mathrm{can}
. For
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{S}
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}})\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{fib}(\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}})\xrightarrow{\varphi-can}\operatorname{map}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{fib}(\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq s}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{s\}[r]\xrightarrow{\varphi\{s\}-\mathrm{can}\{s\}}R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{s\}[r])

\displaystyle=R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(s))[r].

Since
\varphi
and
\mathrm{can}
are maps in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
, and the forgetful functor

\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p}))\to\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})

preserves all limits and all limits are representable in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
by
[
Lur17
, Corollary 3.2.2.5]
, we conclude.
∎

3.3.
Consequences of motivic representability

We now list some immediate result following from the fact that
\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}},\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
,
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}}
,
\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}},\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}
are oriented ring spectra in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}
.

Theorem 3.17
.

Let
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}
. Let
X
be a smooth scheme over
S
and
Z\subseteq X
a closed subscheme of relative codimension
d
, such that
Z\to X\to S
is smooth. Let
{\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)
denote the blow-up of
X
in
Z
and
E
be the exceptional divisor, so that
({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S}
. For all
j
, there are Gysin maps, functorial in
(X,Z)
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{gys}_{Z/X}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\colon

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(Z_{p}^{\wedge})\{j-d\}[-2d]\to R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X_{p}^{\wedge})\{j\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{gys}_{Z/X}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\colon

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j-d}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(Z_{p}^{\wedge})\{j-d\}[-2d]\to\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X_{p}^{\wedge})\{j\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{gys}_{Z/X}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}}\colon

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(Z_{p}^{\wedge},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j-d))[-2d]\to R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(X_{p}^{\wedge},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j))

in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. The homotopy cofibers are respectively given as

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge})\{j\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge})\{j\}

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j))

If
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}_{R}
with
R
perfectoid, we have Gysin maps for the non-completed prismatic cohomology relative to
R
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{gys}_{Z/X}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}\colon

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(Z_{p}^{\wedge}/R)\{j-d\}[-2d]\to R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(X_{p}^{\wedge}/R)\{j\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{gys}_{Z/X}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}\colon

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j-d}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(Z_{p}^{\wedge}/R)\{j-d\}[-2d]\to\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(X_{p}^{\wedge/R})\{j\}

in
\mathcal{D}(A_{\inf}(R))
. The homotopy cofibers are respectively given as

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge}/R)\{j\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge}/R)\{j\}

Proof.

Immediate from (
2.18.2
).
∎

Remark 3.18
.

When
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}_{R}
for
R
a perfectoid ring, the Gysin sequences above take values in
\mathcal{D}(A_{\inf}(R))
. In that case, after base-change along
\theta
, the equivalence (
3.15.1
) exchanges the prismatic Gysin sequence with the analogous sequence for de Rham cohomology, as both come from homotopy purity.

Remark 3.19
.

More general log structures on
X
and
Z
are allowed, in light of
[
BPØ22b
, Theorem 7.5.4]
and
[
BPØ23
, Proposition 7.3.9]
, see
[
BLPØ23a
, Theorem 9.4]
. More precisely, the Gysin maps can be constructed in the following generality:
Let
D_{1},D_{2},\ldots,D_{r}
be smooth divisors on
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{Sm}}_{S}
, forming a strict normal crossing divisor
D
over
S
. Let
Z
be a smooth closed subscheme of
X
of pure codimension
d
, having strict normal crossings with
D_{1}+\ldots+D_{r}
over
S
and not contained in any component of
D_{1}\cup\ldots D_{r}
. Let
Y=(X,D)
(resp.
W=(Z,Z\cap D)
be the log scheme in
\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{S}
given by the compactifying log structure
X-D\hookrightarrow X
(resp.
Z-(Z\cap D)\hookrightarrow Z
). Let
E
be the exceptional divisor in
{\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)
, and consider the log scheme
({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(Y),E)
, given by the compactifying log structure

{\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)-(E\cup W_{1}\cup\ldots\cup W_{r})\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)

where
W_{i}
is the strict transform of
D_{i}
. For all
j
, there are fiber sequences

R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(W^{\wedge}_{p})\{j-d\}[-2d]\to R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(Y^{\wedge}_{p})\{j\}\to R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(Y),E)^{\wedge}_{p})\{j\}

in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and similarly for the filtered version, for syntomic cohomology, and for the non-complete version over a perfectoid.

Remark 3.20

(Comparison with Tang’s cycle class map)
.

In
[
Tan22
, Theorem 1.4]
, Tang constructs a Gysin map (called “cycle class map” in
loc. cit.
) in (derived) syntomic cohomology

\mathrm{cyc}_{Y/X}\colon R\Gamma_{\mathrm{syn}}(Y,\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j-d))[-2d]\to R\Gamma_{\mathrm{syn}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j))

for
Y\to X
a regular immersion of codimension
r
, via weighted deformation to the normal cone. We expect this map to agree with our Gysin map
\mathrm{gys}_{Y/X}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}}
when both are defined.
We remark that the argument of
[
Tan22
]
does not allow, to the best of our understanding, to identify the cofiber of
{\rm cyc}_{Y/X}
explicitly.

We can also use motivic methods to compute the prismatic and syntomic cohomology of Grassmanians. Write
\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)
for the Grassmannian classifying
r
-dimensional subspaces of an
n
-dimensional vector space, defined over
\mathbb{Z}
. We consider
\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)
as a log scheme over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
, with trivial log structure.
As in
[
BPØ23
, §7.4]
, we let :

R_{n,d}=\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1},\ldots,y_{d}]/(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})

with
z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}
satisfying

1+tz_{1}+\ldots+t^{n}z_{n}=(1+tx_{1}+\ldots+t^{r}x_{r})(1+ty_{1}+\ldots+t^{n-r}y_{n-r}).

As a ring, it agrees with the singular cohomology of the complex Grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)(\mathbb{\mathbf{C}})
(see
[
NSØ09
, Section 6.2]
for another description).

Theorem 3.21
.

Let
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}
. Let
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{S}
. There are isomorphisms of bigraded rings, functorial in
X
:

\displaystyle\varphi_{r,n}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\colon H^{*}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X^{\wedge}_{p})\{\bullet\}\otimes{Z}R_{r,n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{*}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}((\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\times X)_{p}^{\wedge})\{\bullet\}

\displaystyle\varphi_{r,n}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\colon\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}H^{*}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X^{\wedge}_{p})\{\bullet\}\otimes{Z}R_{r,n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq\bullet}H^{*}_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}((\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\times X)_{p}^{\wedge})\{\bullet\}

\displaystyle\varphi_{r,n}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}}\colon H^{*}_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(X^{\wedge}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\bullet))\otimes{Z}R_{r,n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{*}_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}((\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\times X)_{p}^{\wedge},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\bullet)).

If
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}_{R}
with
R
perfectoid, a similar statement holds for the non-completed prismatic cohomology relative to
R
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of
[
BPØ23
, Theorem 7.4.2]
, together with the fact that the motivic ring spectra
\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}},\mathbf{E}^{\operatorname{Fil}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
,
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fsyn}}
are oriented.
∎

Finally, by applying the smooth blow-up formula (
2.19.4
) to the aforementioned ring spectra, we have:

Theorem 3.22
.

(Blow-up formula) Let
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}
. Let
X
be a smooth scheme over
S
and let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subscheme such that
Z\to X\to S
is smooth. Then there are equivalences, functorial in
(X,Z)
,

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X^{\wedge}_{p})\{j\}\oplus\bigoplus_{0<i<d}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(Z^{\wedge}_{p})\{j-i\}[-2i]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)^{\wedge}_{p})\{j\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X^{\wedge}_{p})\{j\}\oplus\bigoplus_{0<i<d}\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq j-i}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(Z^{\wedge}_{p})\{j-i\}[-2i]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq j}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\{j\}({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)^{\wedge}_{p})

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(X^{\wedge}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j))\oplus\bigoplus_{0<i<d}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}(Z^{\wedge}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j-i))[-2i]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Fsyn}}({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)^{\wedge}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j))

If
S\in\operatorname{QSyn}_{R}
with
R
perfectoid, a similar statement holds for the non-completed prismatic cohomology relative to
R
.

4.
Saturated descent and crystalline comparison

The goal of this section is to prove that, under certain conditions, Gabber’s cotangent complex is controlled by the classical, non-logarithmic cotangent complex. In light of the secondary filtration, the same is true for log prismatic cohomology of log quasisyntomic rings over a perfectoid ring (see Theorem
4.18
).
Similar descent results are enjoyed by the log de Rham-Witt complex of
[
Yao21
]
, and are a key tool in the proof of the crystalline comparison Theorems
4.30
and
4.31
.

4.1.
Saturated descent for the cotangent complex

Fix a prime number
p
. Recall that for any commutative monoid
M
, we have the
p
-power map (we will use the additive notation for monoids in this section)

F_{M}\colon M\to M,\quad x\mapsto px

Definition 4.1
.

We say that the monoid
M
is
perfect
(resp.
semiperfect
) if
F_{M}
is an isomorphism (resp. if
F_{M}
is surjective).

The two notions admit a relative version given as follows. We write
M_{\rm perf}
(resp.
M^{\flat}
) for the direct (resp. inverse) limit perfection (see
[
BLPØ23b
, §4.5]
).

Definition 4.2

(Relative Frobenius)
.

Let
P\to M
be a map of monoids.
Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{(1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{M/P}}

where the left square is defined to be cocartesian. Here
F_{P}
and
F_{M}
denote the
p
-Frobenius endomorphism of
P
and
M
respectively (see
[
Ogu18
, §I.4.4]
).
We say that the map
P\to M
is
relatively perfect
(resp.
relatively semiperfect
) if the relative Frobenius
F_{M/P}
is an isomorphism (resp. surjective).

Recall that a monoid
M
is
saturated
if it is integral (i.e.
M\subseteq M^{\rm gp}
), and for all
x\in M^{\rm gp}
there is
n>0
such that
nx\in M
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
M
be a semiperfect saturated monoid.
Then the inclusion
M^{*}\to M
is relatively perfect.
In particular,
if
M
is a semiperfect sharp saturated monoid,
then
M
is perfect.

Proof.

Let
(x,y)
and
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
be two elements of
M\oplus_{M^{*},F_{M^{*}}}M^{*}=M^{(1)}
.
If
F_{M/M^{*}}(x,y)=F_{M/M^{*}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
,
then we have
px+y=px^{\prime}+y^{\prime}
.
This implies
p(x-x^{\prime})\in M^{*}
,
so
x-x^{\prime}\in M^{*}
, since
M
is saturated.
Hence
(x,y)=(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
in
M^{(1)}
.
This shows that
F_{M/M^{*}}
is injective.
Since
F_{M}
is surjective by assumption,

F_{M/M^{*}}
is surjective.
∎

We now state a key result that will be very useful in the rest of the section. The proof is the same as
[
Ill02
, Lemma 3.3]
, but for the sake of completeness, we spell it out in the generality that we need. Recall
[
Ogu18
, Definition I.4.3.1]
that a morphism
u\colon P\to Q
of integral monoids is Kummer if it is injective and if, for every
q\in Q
there exists
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
and
p\in P
such that
nq=u(p)
(this last condition is also known as being
\mathbb{Q}
-surjective).

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
u\colon P\to Q
be a Kummer map of saturated monoids.
Then the map

Q\oplus_{P}^{\rm sat}Q\to Q\oplus Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp}\qquad(a,b)\mapsto(ab,\overline{b})

is an isomorphism of monoids.
The left-hand side is the pushout in the category of saturated monoids and
\overline{(\ )}
is the natural map
Q\hookrightarrow Q^{\rm gp}\to Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp}
. More generally, we have an isomorphism

(4.4.1)

Q^{\oplus^{\rm sat}_{P}d}\cong Q\oplus(Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp})^{d-1}

for all
d\geq 1
.

Proof.

The second part follows from the first by induction on
d
since

Q^{\oplus^{\rm sat}_{P}d}\cong Q\oplus^{\rm sat}_{P}(Q^{\oplus^{\rm sat}_{P}d-1})\cong Q\oplus^{\rm sat}_{P}(Q\oplus(Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp})^{d-2})\cong(Q\oplus_{P}^{\rm sat}Q)\oplus(Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp})^{d-1}).

We follow
[
Ill02
, Lemma 3.3]
. Recall that
Q\oplus_{P}^{\rm sat}Q
is the submonoid of
(Q\oplus_{P}Q)^{\rm gp}\cong Q^{\rm gp}\oplus_{P^{\rm gp}}Q^{\rm gp}
given by pairs
(a,b)
such that there is
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
with
ma,mb\in Q
. On the other hand,
Q^{\rm gp}\oplus_{P^{\rm gp}}Q^{\rm gp}
is also a pushout in the category of abelian groups, so there is an isomorphism
Q^{\rm gp}\oplus_{P^{\rm gp}}Q^{\rm gp}\simeq Q^{\rm gp}\oplus Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp}
given by the map
(a,b)\mapsto(ab,\overline{b})
. In particular, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q\oplus^{\rm sat}_{P}Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q\oplus Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Q\oplus_{P}Q)^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\rm gp}\oplus_{P^{\rm gp}}Q^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\rm gp}\oplus Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({a,b)\mapsto(ab,\overline{b})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a,b)\mapsto(ab,\overline{b})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

which implies that (
4.4.1
) is injective. Let
(x,y)\in Q\oplus Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp}
, then since
P\to Q
is
\mathbb{Q}
-surjective there is
n>0
such that
ny=0
, so
n(x,y)=(nx,0)
, which is in the image of
Q\oplus^{\rm sat}_{P}Q
, and since it is saturated we have that
(x,y)
is also in the image, showing the surjectivity of (
4.4.1
).
∎

Remark 4.5
.

If
M
is
p
-torsionfree, the map
M\to M_{\rm perf}
is always Kummer: Indeed, it is injective, and for all
x\in M_{\rm perf}
there is
n
such that
{p^{n}}x\in M
, hence the canonical morphism is
\mathbb{Q}
-surjective.
Moreover, if
M
is integral (resp. saturated), then
M_{\rm perf}
is also integral (resp. saturated) by
[
Ogu18
, Proposition I.1.3.6]
.

Remark 4.6
.

Let
P\to Q
be a map of saturated monoids, and let
\mathbb{Z}[Q^{\bullet}]
the cosimplicial ring given by
d\mapsto\mathbb{Z}[Q^{\oplus^{\rm sat}_{P}d}]
, with the usual Čech differentials. In light of Lemma
4.4
, following
[
Niz08
, Lemma 3.28]
we can rewrite it as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[P]\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}\mathbb{Z}[Q]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[Q\oplus Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[Q\oplus(Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp})^{2}]\longrightarrow\ldots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{1}}

where the differentials
b_{k}^{n}\colon\mathbb{Z}[Q\oplus(Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp})^{n}]\to\mathbb{Z}[Q\oplus(Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp})^{n+1}]
are determined (in multiplicative notation) by

b_{k}^{n}(x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\begin{cases}(x_{0},\overline{x_{0}}x_{1}^{-1}x_{2}^{-1}\cdots x_{n}^{-1},x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})&\text{ if $k=0$}\\
(x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{k-1},1,x_{k},\ldots,x_{n})\quad&\text{ if $k\neq 0$}\end{cases}

and, as above,
\overline{x_{0}}
denotes the image of
x_{0}\in Q
in
Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp}
.
By (the proof of)
[
Niz08
, Lemma 3.28]
we have that
\mathbb{Z}[P]\to\mathbb{Z}[Q\oplus(Q^{\rm gp}/P^{\rm gp})^{\bullet}]
is a chain homotopy equivalence, and therefore the same is true for
\mathbb{Z}[P]\to\mathbb{Z}[Q^{\bullet}]
.

The following is a generalization of
[
BLPØ23b
, Corollary 4.18]
, providing sufficient conditions under which the
p
-completed log cotangent complex agrees with its non-log (classical) counterpart.

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
R\to(R,P)\to(A,M)
be pre-log rings with
P
and
M
saturated.

(1)

If
M
is semiperfect, the map

(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{A/R})\to(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R})

is an equivalence after derived
p
-completion.

(2)

If
P
is semiperfect, the map

(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R})\rightarrow(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,P)})

is an equivalence after derived
p
-completion.

In particular, if both
P
and
M
are semiperfect the composite map

(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{A/R})_{p}^{\wedge}\xrightarrow{}(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,P)})_{p}^{\wedge}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let
M
be semiperfect. By derived Nakayama, it is enough to prove that the maps are equivalences after applying
-\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
.
By Lemma
4.3
, we can use
[
BLPØ23b
, Lemma 4.17]
(which is essentially
[
Bha12b
, Corollary 7.11]
) and
obtain equivalences

\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[M]/\mathbb{F}_{p}[M^{*}]}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{(\mathbb{F}_{p}[M],M)/\mathbb{F}_{p}[M^{*}]}\simeq 0.

The two transitivity sequences

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[M^{*}]/\mathbb{F}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{F}_{p}[M]\to\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[M]/\mathbb{F}_{p}}\to\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[M]/\mathbb{F}_{p}[M^{*}]}

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[M^{*}]/\mathbb{F}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{F}_{p}[M]\to\mathbb{L}_{(\mathbb{F}_{p}[M],M)/\mathbb{F}_{p}}\to\mathbb{L}_{(\mathbb{F}_{p}[M],M)/\mathbb{F}_{p}[M^{*}]}

induce an equivalence

\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[M]/\mathbb{F}_{p}}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{(\mathbb{F}_{p}[M],M)/\mathbb{F}_{p}}.

From this,
we obtain an equivalence

\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]/\mathbb{Z}}\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{(\mathbb{Z}[M],M)/\mathbb{Z}}\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}

since
\mathbb{Z}[M]\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\simeq\mathbb{F}_{p}[M]
.
We have a cocartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]/\mathbb{Z}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{A/R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{(\mathbb{Z}[M],M)/\mathbb{Z}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}}

by
[
BLPØ23a
, (3.3)]
,
so we deduce

\mathbb{L}_{A/R}\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
, in particular
\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/A}\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\simeq 0
. Then the transitivity sequence

\mathbb{L}_{A/R}\to\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}\to\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/A}

induces a finite filtration on
\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}
with graded pieces given by

\operatorname{gr}^{j}(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R})\simeq\bigwedge^{j}\mathbb{L}_{A/R}\otimes^{L}_{A}\bigwedge^{i-j}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/A}.

By applying
-\otimes^{L}{Z}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
we deduce that
\operatorname{gr}^{j}
vanishes for
j<i
and for
j=i
it gives the first desired equivalence

\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{A/R}\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\simeq\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}.

Let now
P
be semiperfect, consider the transitivity sequence

\mathbb{L}_{(R,P)/R}\otimes_{R}^{L}A\to\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}\to\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,P)}.

By the previous case, we have
\mathbb{L}_{(R,P)/R}\otimes^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\simeq 0
,
hence again by looking at the filtration on the derived exterior powers, we conclude that

\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\simeq\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,P)}\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}

as required.
∎

Corollary 4.8
.

Let
\varphi\colon(R,P)\to(A,M)
be a map of saturated pre-log rings with
P
and
M
semiperfect.
Then
\varphi
is log quasismooth (resp. quasisyntomic) if and only if
\underline{\varphi}\colon R\to A
is quasismooth (resp. quasisyntomic).

Proof.

Immediate from Proposition
4.7
and the definition of quasismooth/log quasismooth (resp. quasisyntomic/log quasisyntomic), see
[
BLPØ23b
, Definition 4.5]
and
[
BMS19
, Definition 4.10(2, 3)]
.
∎

Let
\Delta
be the cosimplicial category. Recall that if
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
is a monoidal
\infty
-category, then the category of cosimplicial objects
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta,\mathcal{C}^{\otimes})
is again a monoidal
\infty
-category with tensor product defined levelwise
(A\otimes B)^{[i]}=A^{[i]}\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}B^{[i]}
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
(A,M)
be a pre-log ring with
M
saturated, and let
M\to N
be a Kummer map with
N
saturated. Let
\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet}=\check{C}^{\rm sat}_{M}(N)^{\bullet}
be the cosimplicial monoid given by the Čech nerve in the category of saturated monoids of the map
M\to N
. Then

\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet}],\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})/(A,M)}

vanishes in
\mathcal{D}(A)
.

Proof.

By
[
BLPØ23a
, Proposition 3.8]
, we have that

\mathbb{L}_{(\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}],N^{d})/(\mathbb{Z}[M],M)}\simeq\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}]\otimes^{L}{Z}(N^{d})^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}

for all
d\geq 1
. The map
M^{\rm gp}\to(N^{d})^{\rm gp}
is the diagonal. From this, we obtain an equivalence

(4.9.1)

\mathbb{L}_{(\mathbb{Z}[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet}],\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})/(\mathbb{Z}[M],M)}\simeq\mathbb{Z}[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N^{\bullet})]\otimes^{L}{Z}(\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp},

in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta,\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}[M]))
, where the tensor product is taken levelwise.
We now apply the equivalence
N^{\oplus_{M}^{\rm sat}d}\simeq N\oplus(N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})^{\oplus d-1}
of Lemma
4.4
to obtain an equivalence

(\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}\simeq(N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})^{\oplus\bullet}.

in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta,\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}))
.
The right-hand side is the cosimplicial abelian group

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}\oplus N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}\oplus N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}\oplus N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}\ldots,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id\oplus 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\oplus id}

which is chain homotopic to
0
via the map
N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}\to 0
. This implies that the cosimplicial
\mathbb{Z}[M]
-module (
4.9.1
) is chain homotopic to zero.
Base-change for the Gabber cotangent complex provides an equivalence

\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet}],\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})/(A,M)}\simeq A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N^{\bullet})]\otimes^{L}{Z}(\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}

in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta,\mathcal{D}(A))
.
Here
A\in\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta,\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}[M]))
is the constant functor and the tensor product is taken in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta,\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}[M]))
. By the Eilenberg–Zilber theorem, taking the associated double complex we have a quasi–isomorphism of double complexes:

0\simeq A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N^{\bullet})]\otimes^{L}{Z}(\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}

Recall that the homotopy limit of cosimplicial chain complexes agrees with the product totalization of the associated double complex by
[
BK72
, Proposition 4.9]
, and since the double complex associated to
A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N^{\bullet})]\otimes^{L}{Z}(\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}
is an acyclic right-half plane double complex, by
[
Wei94
, Acyclic Assembly Lemma 2.7.3]
we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet}],\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})/(A,M)})

\displaystyle\simeq{\operatorname{Tot}}(A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N^{\bullet})]\otimes^{L}{Z}(\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})

\displaystyle\simeq 0,

as required.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
(R,P)\to(A,M)
be a map of integral pre-log rings with
M
saturated, and let
M\to N
be a Kummer map of saturated monoids. Then the canonical map

\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,P)}\to\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet}],\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})/(R,P)})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Write
N^{\bullet}
for
\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet}
to shorten the notation.
As in
[
BLPØ23a
, Theorem 2.9]
, the transitivity sequence for the composition
(R,P)\to(A,M)\to(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}],N^{\bullet})
gives a cofiber sequence

\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,P)}\to\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}],N^{\bullet})/(R,P)}\to\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}],N^{\bullet})/(A,M)}

in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta,\mathcal{D}(A))
. This induces a finite filtration on the cosimplicial complex of
A
-modules
\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}],N^{\bullet})/(R,P)}
with graded pieces

\mathrm{gr}^{j}(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}],N^{\bullet})/(R,P)})\simeq\bigwedge^{j}(\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}]\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,P)})\otimes^{L}_{A}\bigwedge^{i-j}\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}],N^{\bullet})/(A,M)}.

For
j<i
, we have
\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta^{\rm op}}\bigwedge^{i-j}\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}],N^{\bullet})/(A,M)}\simeq 0
by Lemma
4.9
above, while for
j=i
we have

\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}],N^{\bullet})/(R,P)}\simeq\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}]\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,P)}.

By Remark
4.6
, the map
\mathbb{Z}[M]\to\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}]
is a homotopy equivalence, hence

\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}],N^{\bullet})/(R,P)}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,P)}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}]\simeq\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,P)},

as required.
∎

The following definition is inspired by
[
BMS19
, Definition 4.1]
.

Definition 4.11
.

Let
A
be a ring and let
C\in\mathcal{D}(A)
. We say that
C
is
p
-completely discrete
if
C\otimes^{L}_{A}A/p\in\mathcal{D}(A/p)
is concentrated in degree
0
.

We remark that if
C
is
p
-completely flat in the sense of
[
BMS19
, Definition 4.1(2)]
, then it is
p
-completely discrete in the above sense.

From Lemma
4.10
we get the following descent result for the cotangent complex.

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
(R,P)\to(A,M)
be an integral map of pre-log rings with bounded
p
-power torsion and let

M\to N
be a Kummer map of saturated monoids.
Assume that
A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N]

is
p
-completely discrete.
Then for all
(A,M)\to(S,Q)
with
A\to S

p
-completely
flat and
S
with bounded
p
-power torsion, the natural map

(4.12.1)

(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(S,Q)/(R,P)})_{p}^{\wedge}\to\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{m}\rangle,Q\oplus_{M}\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{m})/(R,P)})_{p}^{\wedge}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

As before, we write
N^{d}
for
\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{d}
.
As in
[
BMS19
, Remark 4.9]
, it is enough to check that the natural map

(4.12.2)

\mathbb{L}_{(S/p^{n},Q)/(R,P)}\to\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}(\mathbb{L}_{(S/p^{n}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}],Q\oplus_{M}N^{m})/(R,P)})

is an equivalence for all
n
. We can thus assume that
p
is nilpotent in
A
, so
A\to S
is flat and the map

(4.12.3)

A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N]\to A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N]

is an equivalence.
Now, we have that for all
d

\displaystyle S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}]

\displaystyle\cong S\otimes_{A}A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N]\otimes{Z}\mathbb{Z}[(N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})^{\oplus d-1}]

\displaystyle\overset{(*1)}{\simeq}S\otimes_{A}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N]\otimes{Z}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[(N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})^{\oplus d-1}])

\displaystyle\overset{(*2)}{\simeq}S\otimes_{A}^{L}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}])

\displaystyle\simeq S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}]

where
(*1)
is (
4.12.3
)
and the fact that
\mathbb{Z}[N]
is flat over
\mathbb{Z}
and
(*2)
is Lemma
4.4
together with the flatness of
S
over
A
(remember that now we are assuming that
p
is nilpotent, so
p
-completely flat is flat).
Observe that

S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}]\simeq S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[Q]}\mathbb{Z}[Q\oplus_{M}N^{d}],

so that we may consider the animated pre-log rings
(S\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}],Q\oplus_{M}N^{d})
.

By the transitivity sequence for the composites
(R,P)\to(S,M)\to(S,Q)
and
(R,P)\to(S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}],N^{d})\to(S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}],Q\oplus_{M}N^{d})
, we have a commutative diagram:

(4.12.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{(S,M)/(R,P)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{(S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}],N^{m})/(R,P)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{(S,Q)/(R,P)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{(S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}],Q\oplus_{M}N^{m})/(R,P)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{(S,Q)/(S,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{(S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}],Q\oplus_{M}N^{m})/S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}])}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the top horizontal arrow is an equivalence in light of Lemma
4.10
, so it is enough to check that the bottom horizontal arrow of (
4.12.4
) is an equivalence. By
[
BLPØ23a
, Corollary 3.9]
, we have a commutative diagram where the columns are fiber sequences

(4.12.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\otimes{Z}Q^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}S\otimes{Z}(Q\oplus_{M}N^{m})^{\rm gp}/(N^{m})^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{(S,Q)/(S,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{(S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}],Q\oplus_{M}N^{m})/S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}])}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{S/S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[Q]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{(S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}]/S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}]\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}]}\mathbb{Z}[Q\oplus_{M}N^{m}])}}

Since the map
N^{d}\to Q\oplus_{M}N^{d}
is constructed as
M\oplus_{M}N^{d}\to Q\oplus_{M}N^{d}
, with the identity on the second factor, so by Lemma
4.4
we have that

\displaystyle(Q\oplus_{M}N^{d})^{\rm gp}/(N^{d})^{\rm gp}

\displaystyle\cong(Q^{\rm gp}\oplus(N^{d})^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})/(0\oplus N^{d})^{\rm gp}

\displaystyle\cong(Q^{\rm gp}\oplus(N^{d})^{\rm gp}/(0\oplus N^{d})^{\rm gp})/M^{\rm gp}\cong Q^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp},

so the top horizontal map of (
4.12.5
) is an equivalence. Finally, we have that the homotopy pushout diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\otimes_{Z[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[Q]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\otimes_{Z[M]}\mathbb{Z}[Q]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}]}

implies that the map

\mathbb{L}_{S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[Q]/S}\to\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{(S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}]/S\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}]\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}]}\mathbb{Z}[Q\oplus_{M}N^{m}])}

equals the map

\mathbb{L}_{S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[Q]/S}\to\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}\mathbb{Z}[N^{m}]\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{L}_{S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[Q]/S}

By Remark
4.6
, the map
\mathbb{Z}[M]\to\mathbb{Z}[N^{\bullet}]
is a homotopy equivalence, so the map above is an equivalence. By the transitivity sequences of the compositions

\displaystyle S\to

\displaystyle S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[Q]\to S

\displaystyle S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}]\to

\displaystyle S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[Q]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}]\to S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N^{d}],

we conclude that the bottom horizontal arrow of (
4.12.5
) is an equivalence too, so we conclude that the bottom horizontal arrow of (
4.12.4
) is an equivalence too. This concludes the proof.
∎

Example 4.13
.

Here we give some examples of
(A,M)
and
M\to N
such that
A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N]
is
p
-completely discrete.

(1)

Let
R
be a ring. Then for
M\to N
any map of monoids, we have that the following squares are (homotopy) cocartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[M]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[N]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R[M]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R[N].}

This implies that we have equivalences

R[N]\simeq R[M]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[N]\simeq R[M]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N]

In particular,
(R[M],M)
is such that
R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[N]
has
p
-complete Tor-amplitude in degree
0
for all
M\to N
.

(2)

Let
(A,M)
be a saturated pre-log
R
-algebra.
Then
M\to M_{\rm perf}
is Kummer and
M_{\rm perf}
is saturated, as observed in Remark
4.5
.
Assume that
R[M]\to A
is
p
-completely flat, then

\displaystyle A/p\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[M_{\rm perf}]

\displaystyle\simeq A/p\otimes_{R/p[M]}R/p[M_{\rm perf}]

\displaystyle\simeq A/p\otimes_{R/p[M]}^{L}R/p[M_{\rm perf}]\simeq A/p\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[M_{\rm perf}].

This implies that
A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[M_{\rm perf}]
is
p
-completely discrete.

(3)

Let
(R,\mathbb{N})
be the pre-log ring with structure map
m_{0}\colon\mathbb{N}\to R
given by

r\mapsto\begin{cases}1&\mathrm{if}\>\>\>r=0\\
0&\mathrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

This factors through a map
\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}\to R
. Let
(A,M)
be a saturated pre-log
(R,\mathbb{N})
-algebra. Notice that the commutativity of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

of monoids implies that the map
\mathbb{N}\to M
is injective. Indeed, if
r\in\mathbb{N}
such that
f(r)=1
in
M
, then
1=mf(r)=\varphi m_{0}(r)
, so
r=0
.

Let us moreover assume that
R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}]}\mathbb{Z}[M]\to A

p
-completely flat and that
\mathbb{N}\to M
is a saturated morphism of monoids. Let
M^{\prime}:=M\oplus_{\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{{\operatorname{Id}}\otimes F_{\mathbb{N}}}M\oplus_{\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{N}
. Since it is a filtered colimit of saturated monoids it is again saturated, and
M^{\prime}_{\rm perf}\simeq M_{\rm perf}
, which is saturated and
M^{\prime}\to M_{\rm perf}
is Kummer by Remark
4.5
. Moreover, the map
\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}\to M^{\prime}
is injective and saturated since the colimit is filtered, so by
[
Kat88
, Proposition 4.1]
the map
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]\to\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]
is flat. Finally, we have that
R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}]}\mathbb{Z}[M]\cong R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]
, so
R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]\to A
is also
p
-completely flat. Putting everything together, we have that

\displaystyle A/p\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]}\mathbb{Z}[M_{\rm perf}]

\displaystyle\cong A/p\otimes_{R/p\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]}R/p\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]}\mathbb{Z}[M_{\rm perf}]

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\simeq}A/p\otimes_{R/p\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]}^{L}R/p\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[M_{\rm perf}]

\displaystyle\simeq A/p\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[M_{\rm perf}],

where the derived tensors that appear in
(*)
follow respectively from the assumption that
A
is
p
-completely flat over
R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]
, from the fact that
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]\to\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]
is flat, and from (1) above. From this, we deduce that
A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[M^{\prime}]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}[M_{\rm perf}]
is
p
-completely concentrated in degree
0
.

4.2.
Saturated descent for prismatic cohomology

Proposition 4.14
.

Let
A
be a quasisyntomic ring. For all pre-log rings
(A,M)
with
M
semiperfect, the canonical map
A\to(A,M)
induces an isomorphism

\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{A}\simeq\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}.

Proof.

Consider a quasisyntomic cover
A\to S
with
S\in\operatorname{QRSPerfd}
. As observed in Corollary
4.8
,
(A,M)\to(S,M)
is a quasisyntomic cover. It thus suffices to check that
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S/R}\simeq\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq m}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(S,M)/R}
for
R\to S
a map with
R
perfectoid.
By the secondary filtration, it is enough to check it on the graded pieces, so it is enough to check that the map

(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{S/R})_{p}^{\wedge}\to(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(S,M)/R})_{p}^{\wedge}

is an equivalence. This follows directly from Proposition
4.7
.
∎

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
M
be any monoid and let
A
be
p
-complete with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion. Then
\mathbb{L}_{(A\langle M\rangle,M)/A}\simeq A\langle M\rangle\otimes{Z}^{L}M^{\rm gp}[0]
after derived
p
-completion. In particular, the map
A\to(A\langle M\rangle,M)
is log quasisyntomic.

Proof.

Since
A
has bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion and
A[M]
is a free
A
-module,
A\to A\langle M\rangle
is
p
-completely faithfully flat by
[
BMS19
, Lemma 4.4]
, and it is classically
p
-complete concentrated in degree zero with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion by
[
BMS19
, Lemma 4.7]
. By
[
BLPØ23b
, Remark 4.13]
, we have

\mathbb{L}_{(A\langle M\rangle,M)/A}\simeq A\langle M\rangle\otimes_{A[M]}^{L}\mathbb{L}_{(A[M],M)/A}

after derived
p
-completion. On the other hand, by
[
BLPØ23a
, Proposition 3.8]
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{(A\langle M\rangle,M)/A}

\displaystyle\simeq A\langle M\rangle\otimes_{A[M]}^{L}(A[M]\otimes^{L}{Z}M^{\rm gp}[0])

\displaystyle\simeq A\langle M\rangle\otimes{Z}^{L}M^{\rm gp}[0].

after derived
p
-completion. Since
A\to A\langle M\rangle
is
p
-completely faithfully flat, we have that

A\langle M\rangle\otimes_{A}^{L}A/p
has
p
-complete Tor amplitude in degree
0
, so

(A\langle M\rangle\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}M^{\rm gp})\otimes_{A}^{L}A/p\simeq(A\langle M\rangle\otimes_{A}A/p)\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}M^{\rm gp}

has Tor amplitude in
[-1,0]
(as the Tor amplitude of any abelian group is
[-1,0]
), which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
(B,P)
be a pre-log ring such that
B
is
p
-complete with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion. Let
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(B,P)}
with
M
sharp and saturated, and let
M\to N
be a map of saturated monoids with
N
semiperfect such that the following conditions hold:

(i)

\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle P\rangle\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle
is
p
-completely flat

(ii)

B\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle P\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle\to A
is
p
-completely flat.

(ii)

B\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}^{L}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle
is
p
-completely discrete.

Then
(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle,N)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(B,P)}
.

Proof.

By
(i)
, we have that
B\to B\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle P\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle
is
p
-completely flat, so the latter has bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion by
[
BMS19
, Corollary 4.8 (1)]
. Moreover, by
(ii)
, the map
B\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle P\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle\to A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle
is
p
-completely flat, so
A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle
has bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion.
By
p
-completing the sequence of
[
BLPØ23a
, Corollary 3.10]
and by
[
BLPØ23b
, Remark 4.13]
, we obtain a cofiber sequence of the form

A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}(M^{\rm gp})_{p}^{\wedge}\to(\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/\mathbb{Z}_{p}})_{p}^{\wedge}\to(\mathbb{L}_{A/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle})_{p}^{\wedge}.

Since
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(B,P)}
,
(\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/\mathbb{Z}_{p}})_{p}^{\wedge}
has
p
-complete Tor amplitude
[-1,0]
in
\mathcal{D}(A)
, and since
M^{\rm gp}
is torsion-free by
[
Ogu18
, Proposition I.1.3.5 (2)]
, the
A
-module
A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}(M^{\rm gp})_{p}^{\wedge}
is
p
-completely flat.
We conclude that
\mathbb{L}_{A/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}
has
p
-completed Tor amplitude
[-1,0]
in
\mathcal{D}(A)
.
Thus the same holds for the pushout
\mathbb{L}_{A/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}^{L}_{A}(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle)

along the map
A\to A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle
by
[
BMS19
, Lemma 4.5]
.
By condition (ii) and base-change for the cotangent complex, this is equivalent to
\mathbb{L}_{A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle}
. Using the cofiber sequence

(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle)\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}(N^{\rm gp})_{p}^{\wedge}\to\mathbb{L}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle,N)/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\to\mathbb{L}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle},

we find that
\mathbb{L}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle,N)/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
has
p
-complete Tor amplitude
[-1,0]
in
\mathcal{D}(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle N\rangle)
.
∎

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
(R,P)
be a pre-log ring with
R
perfectoid and
P
semiperfect, valuative
and sharp. Let
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(R,P)}
with
M
sharp and saturated,
P\to M
exact and
R\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle P\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle\to A

p
-completely flat. Let

M\to N
be a Kummer map of saturated monoids such that
N
is semiperfect and

A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}^{L}R\langle N\rangle
is
p
-completely discrete.
Then the natural
\varphi
-equivariant map of
E_{\infty}
-rings
