(4.17.1)

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)/R}\to\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}({\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet}\rangle,\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet})/R})

is an equivalence, compatible with the Nygaard filtrations.

Proof.

The map is one of
E_{\infty}
-rings, so it is enough to show that the map is an equivalence of objects of
\mathcal{D}(\Lambda)
, as the forgetful functor from
E_{\infty}
-rings is conservative. We again write
N^{\bullet}
for
\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N)^{\bullet}
to shorten the notation.
Since
M\to N
is Kummer and
M
is saturated,
M\to N
is exact by
[
Ogu18
, Proposition I.4.3.5]
. It follows that the map
P\to N
is exact. Since
P
is valuative, it is local by
[
Ogu18
, Proposition I.4.2.1]
, and so the map
\mathbb{Z}[P]\to\mathbb{Z}[M]
is flat by
[
Ogu18
, Propositions I.4.6.3(4) and I.4.6.7]
. Combining this with the assumption that
A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}^{L}R\langle N\rangle
is
p
-completely discrete, we conclude by Lemma
4.16
that
A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle N\rangle\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(R,P)}
.
By quasisyntomic descent
[
BLPØ23b
, Theorem 4.30 and §7.7]
, it is enough to show that for
(A,M)\to(S,Q)
a log quasisyntomic cover with
(S,Q)\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}_{R}
, the map

(4.17.2)

\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq n}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(S,Q)/R}\to\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta^{\rm op}}(\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq n}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(S\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle N^{\bullet}\rangle,Q\oplus_{M}N^{\bullet})/R})

is an equivalence. By Lemma
4.4
, we have that
N^{d}\simeq N\oplus(N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})^{\oplus d-1}
, which induces an isomorphism
R\langle N^{d}\rangle\simeq R\langle N\rangle\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R}R\langle(N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})^{d-1}\rangle
. We thus have that

A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle N^{d}\rangle\cong(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle N\rangle)\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R}R\langle(N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})^{d-1}\rangle,

which is quasisyntomic over
R\langle(N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})^{d-1}\rangle
by
[
BMS19
, Lemma 4.16(2)]
.
In particular, it is quasisyntomic over
R
by Lemma
4.15
.
Since
(A,M)\to(S,Q)
is a quasisyntomic cover,
A\to S
is a quasisyntomic cover, so
S\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle N^{d}\rangle
is quasisyntomic again by
[
BMS19
, Lemma 4.16(2)]
. Moreover,
S/p
and
Q
are semiperfect by the assumption that
(S,Q)\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}_{R}
(see
[
BLPØ23b
, Definition 4.12]
), and
N^{d}\cong N\oplus(N^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})^{\oplus d-1}
is semiperfect since
N
is semiperfect by assumption, so
N^{\rm gp}
is also semiperfect. This implies that
R\langle N^{d}\rangle
is perfectoid by
[
BLPØ23b
, Lemma 4.7]
. We thus have that
S\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle N^{d}\rangle/p
and
Q\oplus_{M}N^{d}
are semiperfect, so that

(S\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle N^{d}\rangle,Q\oplus_{M}N^{d})\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}_{R}.

By
[
BLPØ23b
, Proposition 7.4]
,
we are reduced to check (
4.17.2
) on the finite filtration of the graded pieces of
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq n}
. That is, we are reduced to checking that the map

(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(S,Q)/R})_{p}^{\wedge}\to\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta^{\rm op}}(\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{((S\otimes_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle N^{\bullet}\rangle,Q\oplus_{M}N^{\bullet})/R})_{p}^{\wedge},

is an equivalence for all
i
. But this is precisely Theorem
4.12
.
∎

Theorem 4.18
.

Let
B
be a
p
-complete ring with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion and let
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{B}
with
M
sharp and saturated such that
B\langle M\rangle\to A
is
p
-completely flat. Then there is a
\varphi
-equivariant equivalence of
E_{\infty}
-rings:

(4.18.1)

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}({\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{B\langle M\rangle}B\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{\bullet}\rangle)})

compatible with the Nygaard filtrations.

Proof.

As observed in Example
4.13
,
M\to M_{\rm perf}
is a Kummer map of saturated monoids and
A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[M]}\mathbb{Z}[M_{\rm perf}]
is
p
-completely discrete.
By Lemma
4.16
, since
P=\mathrm{triv}
in this case,
(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{B\langle M\rangle}B\langle M_{\rm perf}\rangle,M_{\rm perf})\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{B}
. As in the proof of Lemma
4.17
, we consider
(A,M)\to(S,Q)
a log quasisyntomic cover with
(S,Q)\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}
and let
R\to S
be a map with
R
perfectoid. Then we have an equivalence (
4.17.2
)

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(S,Q)/R}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}({\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(S\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{\bullet}\rangle,Q\oplus_{M}\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{\bullet})/R}),

compatibly with the Nygaard filtration, which by quasisyntomic descent gives an equivalence

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}({\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{B\langle M\rangle}B\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{\bullet}\rangle,\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{\bullet})}).

By Proposition
4.14
we have

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{B\langle M\rangle}B\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}\rangle,\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m})}\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{B\langle M\rangle}B\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}\rangle)}

for all
m\in\Delta
, noting that each
\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}
is saturated (by construction) and semiperfect. This concludes the proof.
∎

Example 4.19
.

Let
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{B}
. Then by
[
Ogu18
, Theorems III.2.5.5 and IV.3.3.3]
étale locally on
{\underline{X}}
there is a chart
(\operatorname{Spec}(A),M)
with
M
sharp and saturated (see Remark
4.29
later) and
B[M]\to A
flat. In particular, if
\mathfrak{X}
is the
p
-completion of
X
, locally it satisfies the condition in Theorem
4.18
.

Remark 4.20
.

Notice that the right-hand side of (
4.18.1
)
is the classical Nygaard-completed prismatic cohomology (i.e. the rings involved have no log structure), but the system is not a Čech nerve as the product is different!

Remark 4.21
.

A similar descent result has been proved in
[
KY23
]
for the Kummer étale cohomology with
p
inverted. In particular one could deduce a comparison between syntomic and Kummer étale cohomology. We leave this to future work.

We finish by proving saturated descent for the non-completed version of prismatic cohomology. Let
R
be a perfectoid ring and denote again by
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{nc}_{-/R}
the non-Nygaard complete prismatic cohomology of Construction
3.9
.

Corollary 4.22
.

Let
R
be a perfectoid ring and let
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{R}
with
M
sharp and saturated such that
R\langle M\rangle\to A
is
p
-completely flat. Then there is an equivalence

L\Omega_{(A,M)/R}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}L\Omega_{(A\otimes_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}\rangle)}

of
E_{\infty}
-rings, and a
\varphi
-equivariant equivalence

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{(A,M)/R}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}\rangle)/R},

of
E_{\infty}
-rings, where the right-hand side is the prismatic cohomology of
[
BS22
]
.

Proof.

We begin by proving the statement for derived (log) de Rham cohomology.

Since
L\Omega_{(A,M)/R}
is
p
-complete (by definition), it is enough to check that the canonical map to the limit is an equivalence modulo
p
. As in
[
BMS19
, Example 5.12]
, let
\operatorname{Fil}^{\rm conj}_{n}L\Omega_{-/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
denote the conjugate filtration (see
[
Bha12b
]
, the definition is identical to the non-log case, and simply follows from the existence of a canonical filtration on the homotopy-colimit of a simplicial cosimplicial
A
-module).
We have that
\operatorname{Fil}^{\rm conj}_{-1}L\Omega_{-/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}=0
, and the filtration is exhaustive, that is
\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{n}\operatorname{Fil}^{\rm conj}_{n}L\Omega_{-/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\simeq L\Omega_{-/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover we have an identification
\mathrm{gr}_{n}^{\rm conj}L\Omega_{-/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\simeq\bigwedge^{n}(\mathbb{L}_{-/R})[-n]\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
. By Proposition
4.7
and Theorem
4.12
, we deduce by induction that for all
n
that

\operatorname{Fil}^{\rm conj}_{n}L\Omega_{(A,M)/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{[m]\in\Delta}\operatorname{Fil}^{\rm conj}_{n}L\Omega_{(A\otimes_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}\rangle)/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}.

Since
(A\otimes_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}\rangle)
is quasisyntomic for all
m
by Lemma
4.16
, we have that
\operatorname{Fil}^{\rm conj}_{n}L\Omega_{(A\otimes_{R\langle M\rangle}R\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}\rangle)/R}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
takes value in
\mathcal{D}^{\geq-1}
for all
n
,
as each
\mathbb{L}_{-/R}
has
p
-completed Tor amplitude
[-1,0]
: this implies that in this case, the direct product totalization coincides with the direct sum totalization (as in each cosimplicial degree there are only finitely many terms), and since the direct sum totalization commutes with filtered colimits we conclude.

For the second isomorphism, we use the fact that
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{-/R}
is
\xi
-complete, so it is enough to check that the natural map

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{(A,M)/R}\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{[m]\in\Delta}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}\rangle,\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m})}

is an equivalence modulo
\xi
, thus reducing to the case of
L\Omega
. One only needs to notice that, since
M_{\rm perf}
is perfect, we have

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}\rangle,\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m})}\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{(A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle M\rangle}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf})^{m}\rangle}.

This can also be checked by working modulo
\xi
, reducing the statement to derived de Rham cohomology, and then to the cotangent complex as before.
Finally, the fact that (non logarithmic)
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{-/R}
agrees with
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A_{\rm inf}(R)}
is the content of
[
BS22
, Theorem 13.1]
.

∎

4.3.
Saturated descent for de Rham–Witt cohomology and comparison

For this subsection, we assume that
k
is a perfect field equipped with an fs log structure
P
. Let
(A,M)
be a pre-log
(k,P)
-algebra. We shall use the variant of the log de Rham–Witt complex
\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}
of
[
Yao21
]
, equipped with the Nygaard filtration

(\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq i}\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)})^{q}:=\begin{cases}p^{i-q-1}V\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}^{q}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}&{\rm if}\>\>\>q<i\\
\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}^{q}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}&{\rm if}\>\>\>q\geq i.\end{cases}

By
[
BLM21
, Proposition 8.2.1]
, since
\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}
is a saturated Dieudonné complex, there is an isomorphism

(4.22.1)

\operatorname{gr}^{i}_{N}(\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)})\simeq\tau^{\leq i}(\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}/p).

In case
(A,M)\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(k,P)}
with
P\to M
saturated
(or equivalently by
[
Ogu18
, Corollary III.2.5.4]

(k,P)\to(A,M)
is of log-Cartier type, see also the note after
[
Ogu18
, Definition III.2.4.1]
), then by
[
Yao21
, Theorems 3 and 7]
we have equivalences

(4.22.2)

\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}\simeq W\Omega_{(A,M)/(k,P)}\qquad\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}/p\simeq\Omega_{(A,M)/(k,P)},

Recall from
[
BLPØ23a
, §2.10]
that the category
\mathrm{Poly}_{(k,P)}
is defined as the category of polynomial pre-log rings over
(k,P)
. The objects are defined to be the free pre-log algebras: concretely, they are given as polynomial rings
(k[T_{I\coprod J}],P\oplus\mathbb{N}^{I})
, where
I
and
J
are finite sets and
\mathbb{N}^{I}
is the pre-log structure sending the standard basis element
e_{i}\in\prod_{i\in I}\mathbb{N}
to
T_{i}
(see
[
BLPØ23b
, §2.2]
): by construction if
(A,M)\in\mathrm{Poly}_{(k,P)}
, then it is log smooth and saturated.

By left Kan extension from
\mathrm{Poly}_{(k,P)}
, we construct the derived de Rham–Witt complex with its Nygaard filtration on animated pre-log
(k,P)
-algebras similarly to
[
BLM21
, Construction 9.2.5]
, so
\operatorname{gr}_{N}^{i}L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(-)/(k,P)}
has a finite secondary filtration with graded pieces
\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(-)/(k,P)}
.
We immediately deduce the following result:

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
(A,M)
be a quasisyntomic
(k,P)
-algebra with
M,P
saturated and semiperfect. Then we have that

\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq i}L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}\simeq\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq i}L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{A/k}.

Proof.

As in Proposition
4.14
, we reduce to the graded pieces and deduce the result from Proposition
4.7
.
∎

Let
\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(-)/(k,P)}
be the Nygaard completion of
L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(-)/(k,P)}
. By reducing to the filtrations in the usual way, we have that
(\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(-)/(k,P)},\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq\bullet})
is a quasisyntomic sheaf. The following is analogous to Lemma
4.17
.

Lemma 4.24
.

Let
(A,M)
be a quasisyntomic saturated
(k,P)
-algebra and let
M\to N
be a Kummer map of saturated monoids such that
N
is semiperfect and
A\otimes^{L}_{k[M]}k[N]
is discrete.
Consider the Čech nerve
\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N^{\bullet})
of
M\to N
in the category of saturated monoids.
Then the natural
\varphi
-equivariant map of
E_{\infty}
-rings

(4.24.1)

\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}\to\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}(\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{k[M]}k[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N^{\bullet})],\check{C}^{\rm sat}(N^{\bullet}))/(k,P)})

is an equivalence and compatible with the Nygaard filtrations.

Proof.

By reducing to the finite filtration of
\operatorname{gr}_{N}^{i}\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}
, it boils down to (
4.12.2
).
∎

Then the proof of the saturated descent follows immediately as in Theorem
4.18
:

Theorem 4.25
.

Let
k
be a perfect field and let
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{k}
with
M
saturated and such that
k[M]\to A
is flat. Then there is a
\varphi
-equivariant equivalence of
E_{\infty}
-rings:

(4.25.1)

\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A,M)/k}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}(\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A\otimes_{k[M]}k[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet})])/k})

compatible with the Nygaard filtration. ∎

We are now ready to prove our crystalline comparison results. We first deduce the comparison with
P=\mathrm{triv}
.

Theorem 4.26
.

Let
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{k}
with
M
sharp and saturated such that
k[M]\to A
is flat.
Then there is a
\varphi
-equivariant equivalence of filtered
E_{\infty}
-rings

(4.26.1)

\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A,M)/k}\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}

lifting the equivalence
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}/\xi\simeq\widehat{L\Omega}_{(A,M)/k}
. This equivalence is functorial among maps
(A,M)\to(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{k}
and
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
as above.

Proof.

By
[
BMS19
, Theorem 8.17]
(see also
[
Mat22
, Theorem 9.1]
), for every
A
quasisyntomic over
k
(with trivial log structure) there are functorial isomorphisms of filtered
E_{\infty}
-rings

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{A}\simeq\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{A/k}.

in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. By Theorems
4.18
and
4.25
, since
A\otimes_{k[M]}k[M_{\rm perf}^{d}]\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{k}
by Lemma
4.16
, the result follows from the equivalence

(4.26.2)

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{A\otimes_{k[M]}k[M_{\rm perf}^{d}]}\simeq\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{A\otimes_{k[M]}k[M_{\rm perf}^{d}]},

in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, which is functorial since the resolution
A\to A\otimes_{k[M]}k[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet})]
is.
∎

Moreover, we also deduce the following interesting comparison in case
P=\mathbb{N}
, i.e.
(k,{\mathbb{N}})
is the standard log point.

Theorem 4.27
.

Let
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(k,\mathbb{N})}
with
M
sharp and saturated. If
\mathbb{N}\to M
is saturated and
k\otimes_{k[\mathbb{N}]}k[M]\to A
is flat,
then there is a
\varphi
-equivariant equivalence

\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}\simeq\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})/k}\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})}.

This equivalence is functorial among maps
(A,M)\to(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(k,\mathbb{N})}
and
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
as above.

Proof.

By Example
4.13
(3), we have that

(A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})\simeq(A,M)\otimes_{(k,\mathbb{N})}^{L}(k,\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})

so since the map
\mathbb{N}\to k
factors through
\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}
we have that

\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{(A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})/(k,\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{(A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})/k},

where the last equivalence follows from
[
BLPØ23b
, Corollary 4.18]
. Then the first equivalence follows from the secondary filtration.
By Example
4.13
(3), we have that
A\otimes^{L}_{k[M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}k[M_{\rm perf}]
is discrete, so by Lemma
4.24
and Proposition
4.7
we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})/(k,\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}(\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{k[M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}k[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet})],\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet}))/(k,\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}(\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{k[M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}k[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet})])/k}),

Moreover, by
[
Ogu18
, Proposition I.4.2.1 (6)]
, the map
\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}\to M\otimes{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}
is exact. Finally, by direct computation we have that

\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}=\mathbb{Z}[1/p^{\infty}]_{\geq 0}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}[1/p^{\infty}]=(\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})^{\rm gp},

so it is sharp and valuative. By Lemma
4.17
and Proposition
4.14
, we have equivalences

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta}({\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{k[M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}k[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet})],\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet}))})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Delta^{\rm op}}({\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{k[M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}k[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet})])}).

From this, we obtain an isomorphism of simplicial
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules

(4.27.1)

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{k[M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}k[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet})])}\simeq\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{k[M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}k[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet})])/k}

by
[
BMS19
, Theorem 8.17]
. Functoriality follows from the resolution

A\to A\otimes_{k[M\oplus_{\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}]}k[\check{C}^{\rm sat}(M_{\rm perf}^{\bullet})]

being functorial.
∎

We now aim for variants of the crystalline comparison. For this, we restrict to the case of
(A,M)\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(k,P)}
with
P\to M
saturated. Notice that the Nygaard filtration on
\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}
is clearly complete as
p^{i-q-1}V\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}^{q}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}
vanishes for
i\gg 0
. In particular the map of filtered
E_{\infty}
-rings
L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}
induced by any choice of a polynomial resolution of
(A,M)
factors through
\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}
. In fact, we have the following:

Lemma 4.28
.

Let
(A,M)\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(k,P)}
with
P\to M
saturated. Then the canonical map

\widehat{L\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}\xrightarrow{}\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}

is an equivalence of complete filtered
E_{\infty}
-rings.

Proof.

It is enough to check it on the graded pieces of the secondary filtration: in that case, it is the map

\bigwedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}\to\Omega^{i}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}.

By
[
BLPØ23a
, Proposition 4.6]
, since
(k,P)\to(A,M)
is log smooth and integral by hypothesis, we have that
\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}\simeq\Omega^{1}_{(A,M)/(k,P)}
, so the claim follows.
∎

Remark 4.29
.

Let
R
be a ring, let
P
be an fs sharp monoid, and let
(R,P)
be the standard log base with
P\to R
the zero map. Write again
\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(R,P)}
for the category of log smooth and separated fs log schemes of finite type over
(R,P)
. Let
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(R,P)}
and assume that
X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k,P)
is saturated. Since it is integral, it is s-injective at every point, so by
[
Ogu18
, Theorems III.2.5.5 and IV.3.3.3]
, for every point
x\in{\underline{X}}
there is an étale neighborhood
\widetilde{{\underline{X}}}
and a chart
Q
such that
Q
is sharp,
P\to Q
is saturated and
{\underline{X}}\to\operatorname{Spec}(R\otimes_{R[P]}k[Q])
is flat. In particular, we can globalize Theorems
4.26
and
4.27
:

Theorem 4.30
.

For all
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(k,\mathrm{triv})}
we have an equivalence

(4.30.1)

R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X/k)\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(X/W(k)).

of filtered
E_{\infty}
-rings that depends functorially on
X
.

Proof.

Since the log structure on the base is trivial, the map is saturated and s-injective. Since the cotangent complex is insensitive to logification, by the Nygaard and secondary filtrations this holds also for the prismatic and crystalline cohomology of
X
, so in light of Remark
4.29
we can assume that
X
is étale locally
\operatorname{Spec}(A,M)
with
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{k}
with
M
sharp and saturated. So we conclude by combining Theorem
4.26
, Lemma
4.28
,
[
Yao21
, Theorem 3]
and quasisyntomic descent.
∎

Theorem 4.31
.

For all
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{(k,\mathbb{N})}
with
{\underline{X}}
generically reduced, we have an equivalence

(4.31.1)

R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(({\underline{X}},\partial X\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})/k)\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(X/W(k,\mathbb{N}))

of filtered
E_{\infty}
-rings that depends functorially on
X
.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{N}
is valuative, the map
X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k,\mathbb{N})
is integral, and it is saturated if and only if
{\underline{X}}
is generically reduced (Serre’s condition
(R_{0})
) by
[
Tsu19
, Theorem II.4.11]
, so we deduce that
X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k,\mathbb{N})
is saturated. Then by
[
Ogu18
, Proposition I.4.7.5 and Theorem III.2.2.7]
the morphism is also s-integral. As before, we can assume that
X
is étale locally
\operatorname{Spec}(A,M)
with
(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(k,\mathbb{N})}
with
M
sharp and saturated. So we conclude again by combining Theorem
4.27
, Lemma
4.28
,
[
Yao21
, Theorem 3]
and quasisyntomic descent.
∎

Remark 4.32
.

As observed in
[
BL22
, Warning 4.6.2]
, the equivalences (
4.30.1
) and (
4.31.1
) are
not

W(k)
-linear. Indeed, the maps are
F
-semilinear, inducing equivalences

\displaystyle F^{*}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X)\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(X/W(k))

\displaystyle F^{*}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(X\times_{(k,\mathbb{N})}\operatorname{Spec}(k,\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}))\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(X/W(k,\mathbb{N}))

in
\mathcal{D}(W(k))
.

4.4.
Motivic crystalline comparison

Theorem 4.33
.

When restricted to
\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{k}
, the equivalence (
4.26.1
) induces a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear equivalence

\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\simeq\mathbf{E}^{\rm crys}\textrm{ in }\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(k,\mathbb{Z}_{p})

of oriented ring spectra, which becomes
W(k)
-linear after twisting with Frobenius:

F^{*}\mathbf{E}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\simeq\mathbf{E}^{\rm crys}\textrm{ in }\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}}(k,W(k))

Proof.

As recalled for example in
[
BL22
, Example 2.6.4]
, since the base has characteristic
p
the Breuil–Kisin twist is canonically trivialized, so for the graded commutative monoid in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(k,W(k))
given by the collection
\{R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\{i\}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
is equivalent to the (constant) graded commutative monoid
\{R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\}
. This implies that the equivalence (
4.30.1
) induces an equivalence of constant graded commutative monoids, so by Proposition
2.17
it is enough to show that for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{k}
the following diagram commutes in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}_{p})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\mathbf{P}^{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\rm crys}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}

In fact,
c_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
is induced by the prismatic Chern class
c_{1}^{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathcal{O}(1))
and
c_{\rm crys}
is induced by the crystalline Chern class
c_{1}^{\rm crys}(\mathcal{O}(1))
: then the result follows from
[
BL22
, Proposition 7.5.5]
.

To get the second equivalence, just notice that the prismatic Chern class factors through
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{syn}}(-)\to\operatorname{Fil}^{1}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\xrightarrow{\rm can}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)
: in particular it factors through the
F
-semilinear map
\varphi_{1}\colon\operatorname{Fil}^{1}_{N}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)\xrightarrow{\rm can}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)
: this implies that there is a commutative diagram in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(k,W(k))

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(k)(\mathbf{P}^{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{*}R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(-),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\rm crys}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{*}\gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}

then the result is clear.
∎

Remark 4.34
.

This implies that the prismatic Gysin map of
3.17
agrees with the crystalline Gysin map of
[
Gro85
]
(up to
F^{*}
), as both agree with the sequence induced by homotopy purity (see
[
Mer22
, (4.5.2)]
)

5.
A log Segal conjecture

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
p
. In this section, we will prove an analogue of
[
BMS19
, Corollary 8.18]
in the log setting, which is needed to define the Breuil–Kisin cohomology. First, we prove a statement for pre-log algebras over
(k,\mathrm{triv})
, which is independent of the saturated descent of the previous section. To simplify the notation, we will write
\operatorname{THH},\operatorname{TC}^{-}
and
\operatorname{TP}
for their respective
p
-completed versions.

By
[
BMS19
, Proposition 6.3]
,
we have a commutative square of graded rings with vertical isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}\operatorname{TP}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}[u,v]/(uv-p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\sigma,\sigma^{-1}]}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mapsto\sigma,v\mapsto p\sigma^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi-\text{linear}}

As observed in the proof of
[
BMS19
, Proposition 6.4]
, for all
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant
\operatorname{THH}(k)
-modules
\mathcal{F}
,
we have a natural equivalence

\mathcal{F}^{h\mathbb{T}}\otimes_{\operatorname{TC}^{-}(k)}\operatorname{THH}(k)\simeq\mathcal{F}

For a pre-log
k
-algebra
(A,M)
,
we apply this equivalence to
\mathcal{F}=\operatorname{THH}(A,M)
and
\mathcal{F}=\operatorname{THH}(A,M)^{tC_{p}}
to obtain a commutative square with vertical equivalences

(5.0.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{TC}^{-}(A,M)/v}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{TP}(A,M)/p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{THH}(A,M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{THH}(A,M)^{tC_{p}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

The equivalence
\operatorname{THH}(A,M)^{tC_{p}}\simeq\operatorname{TP}(A,M)/p
is compatible with the motivic filtrations of
[
BLPØ23b
, Proposition 7.10]
by checking on log quasiregular semiperfect
k
-algebras, so it gives equivalences

(5.0.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(A,M)\simeq\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(A,M)/v\simeq\operatorname{Fil}^{\geq i}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}/v[2i]\simeq\operatorname{gr}^{i}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}[2i],

(5.0.3)

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(A,M)^{tC_{p}}\simeq\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TP}(A,M)/p\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)}/p[2i]\simeq\widehat{L\Omega}_{(A,M)/k}[2i],

where the last equivalences are stated in
[
BLPØ23b
, §7.2]
, analogous to
[
BMS19
, Theorem 7.2]
for the non-log case.

Proposition 5.1
.

For all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the above map gives an equivalence of sheaves of spectra on
\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}(k)
:

\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(-)\simeq(\tau^{\leq i}\Omega)[2i],

Moreover, under this equivalence, the map
\varphi\colon\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(-)\to\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(-)^{tC_{p}}
is the natural map
(\tau^{\leq i}\Omega)[2i]\to\Omega[2i]
. In particular, for all
(A,M)\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}(k)
the map

\varphi\colon\operatorname{THH}(A,M)\to\operatorname{THH}(A,M)^{tC_{p}}

is an equivalence in degrees
\geq\dim(A)
.

Proof.

Recall that by the secondary filtration
\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(-)
lives naturally in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(via the Dold-Kan correspondence). Composing (
5.0.3
) with the map
\varphi\colon\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(-)\to\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(-)^{tC_{p}}
we obtain a map

\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(-)\to\Omega_{(-)/k}[2i].

of objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(k,\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
By
[
BMS19
, Corollary 8.18]
, the result holds if
M
is the trivial log structure, in particular if
\eta_{A}
is the generic point of
A
we have that
\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(\eta_{A})\simeq(\tau^{\leq i}\Omega_{\eta_{i}})[2i]
. This implies that
\rm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}[-2i]
is generically
-i
-connective, so by
[
BM23
]
, we conclude that
\rm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}[-2i]
is
-i
-connective for the homotopy
t
-structure of
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{DA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(k)
, in particular
\varphi[-2i]
factors as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(-)[-2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(-)^{tC_{p}}[-2i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\geq-i}{\rm gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}(-)^{tC_{p}}[-2i].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi[-2i]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

To conclude, it is enough to check that
\psi
is an equivalence, and
again by
[
BM23
]
, it is enough to check it on
(\eta_{A},\mathrm{triv})
, which follows again from
[
BMS19
, Corollary 8.18]
.
∎

Let us now move to the case of algebras over the log point
(k,\mathbb{N})
. Here, let
(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N})
be the pre-log ring spectrum given by the quotient map
\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{N}]\to\mathbb{S}
: then if
(A,M)
is a pre-log
(k,\mathbb{N})
-algebra we have an induced map of pre-log ring spectra
(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N})\to(A,M)
. Recall that in light of
[
BLPØ23b
, Remark 8.5]
, the spherical log point
(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N})
is a log cyclotomic base in the sense of
[
BLPØ23b
, Definition 8.3]
.

Proposition 5.2
.

For every saturated log smooth map
(k,\mathbb{N})\to(A,M)
of fs pre-log rings
we have a natural equivalence

\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N}))\simeq(\tau^{\leq i}\Omega_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})})[2i].

Moreover,
under this equivalence the map

\varphi\colon\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N}))\to\mathrm{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N}))^{tC_{p}}

is the natural map
\tau^{\leq i}\Omega_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}\to\Omega_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}
.
In particular,
the map

\varphi\colon\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N}))\to\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N}))^{tC_{p}}

is an equivalence in degrees
\gg 0
.

Proof.

The question is étale local on
A
.
Hence by
[
Kat88
, Theorem 3.5]
,
we may assume that

(i)

\mathbb{N}\to M
is injective and saturated,

(ii)

k\otimes_{k[\mathbb{N}]}k[M]\to A
is étale.

Recall that

\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N}))\simeq\operatorname{THH}((A,M))\otimes_{\operatorname{THH}(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N})}\mathbb{S}

is an equivalence of cyclotomic spectra, since
(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N})
is a log cyclotomic base.
Consider the pre-log ring
(A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})=(A,M)\otimes^{L}_{(k,\mathbb{N})}(k,\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})
as in the proof of Theorem
4.27
. Then we have an equivalence

\operatorname{THH}(A,M\oplus_{\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})\simeq\operatorname{THH}(A,M)\otimes_{\operatorname{THH}(k,\mathbb{N})}\operatorname{THH}(k,\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}).

of cyclotomic spectra.
We shall now argue that there is an equivalence of cyclotomic spectra

(5.2.1)

\operatorname{THH}(A,M\oplus_{\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N})).

For this, consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm THH}({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm THH}({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm THH}(k,{\mathbb{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm THH}(k,{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm THH}((k,{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})/({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm THH}(A,M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm THH}(A,M\oplus_{\mathbb{N}}{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm THH}((A,M)/({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{N}}))}

in which every square is defined to be cocartesian. The identification of the lower right-hand corner is a consequence of the outer square being cocartesian. Since both
k
and
{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf}
are perfect, the canonical map
{\rm THH}(k)\to{\rm THH}(k,{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})
is an equivalence by
[
BLPØ23b
, Rmk 3.5]
, while the canonical map
{\rm THH}(k)\to{\rm THH}((k,{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})/({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf}))
is an equivalence since
({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})\to(k,{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})
is strict. It follows that the map
{\rm THH}(k,{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})\to{\rm THH}((k,{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})/({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf}))
is an equivalence, and thus also the map
{\rm THH}(A,M\oplus_{{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathbb{N}}_{\rm perf})\xrightarrow{}{\rm THH}((A,M)/({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{N}}))
is an equivalence of cyclotomic spectra. The latter of these is (
5.2.1
).

By (
5.0.3
), Theorem
4.27
, and (
4.22.2
) we have an equivalence

\operatorname{gr}_{N}^{i}\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N}))^{tC_{p}}\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}/p[2i]\simeq\Omega_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}[2i]

and the induced map
\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N})\to\Omega_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}[2i]
factors via the isomorphism
\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N})\simeq\operatorname{gr}^{i}_{N}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(}A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})\simeq\tau^{\leq i}\Omega_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}[2i]
of (
4.22.1
). Finally, the map

\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})\to\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TP}(A,M\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})

agrees with the inclusion
\operatorname{Fil}_{N}^{\geq i}\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}[2i]\subseteq\operatorname{\mathcal{W}\Omega}_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}[2i]
: this implies that modulo
v
and
p
it is the natural map
\tau^{\leq i}\Omega_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}\to\Omega_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}
. The last statement follows from the fact that
\Omega^{1}_{(A,M)/(k,\mathbb{N})}
is locally free of finite type by
[
Ogu18
, Proposition IV.3.2.1]
.
∎

6.
The log Breuil–Kisin cohomology

We fix the setup of
[
BMS19
, §11]
.
Let
K
be a discretely valued extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with perfect residue field
k
, ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and fix a uniformizer
\varpi\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Let
K_{\infty}
be the
p
-adic completion of
K(\varpi^{1/p^{\infty}})
and let
C
be the completion of an algebraic closure of
K_{\infty}
and
A_{\rm inf}=A_{\rm inf}(\mathcal{O}_{C})
.

Let
\mathfrak{S}=W(k)\llbracket z\rrbracket
. There is a surjective map
\tilde{\theta}:\mathfrak{S}\to\mathcal{O}_{K}
determined by the inclusion
W(k)\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{K}
and
z\mapsto\varpi
. The kernel of this map is generated by an Eisenstein polynomial
E=E(z)\in\mathfrak{S}
for
\varpi
. Let
\varphi
be the endomorphism of
\mathfrak{S}
determined by the Frobenius on
W(k)
and
z\mapsto z^{p}
. We regard
\mathfrak{S}
as a
\varphi
-stable subring of
A_{\rm inf}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
or
A_{\rm inf}
by the Frobenius on
W(k)
and sending
z
to
[\varpi^{\flat}]^{p}
where
\varpi^{\flat}=(\varpi,\varpi^{p},\varpi^{p^{2}},\ldots)\in\mathcal{O}^{\flat}_{K_{\infty}}
is
our chosen compatible system of
p
-power roots of
\varpi
; the resulting map
\mathfrak{S}\to A_{\rm inf}
is faithfully flat and even topologically free (see
[
BMS19
, Lemma 4.30 and its proof]
). Write
\theta=\tilde{\theta}\circ\varphi:\mathfrak{S}\to\mathcal{O}_{K}
. The embedding
\mathfrak{S}\subseteq A_{\rm inf}
is compatible with the
\theta
and
\tilde{\theta}
maps.

This section aims to construct a cohomology theory for log smooth formal schemes that is valued in Breuil–Kisin
\mathfrak{S}
-modules, generalizing
[
BMS19
, Theorem 11.2]
. Namely, we will prove that:

Theorem 6.1
.

To any log smooth affine formal log scheme
\operatorname{Spf}(A,M)/(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathrm{triv})
, one can functorially attach a
(p,z)
-complete
E_{\infty}
-algebra
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)/\mathfrak{S}}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{S})
together with a
\varphi
-linear Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi:{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)/\mathfrak{S}}\to{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)/\mathfrak{S}}
inducing an isomorphism
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)/\mathfrak{S}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\varphi}\mathfrak{S}[1/E]\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)/\mathfrak{S}}[1/E]
, with the following features:

(1)

(de Rham comparison) After scalar extension along
\theta
, there is a functorial isomorphism

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)/\mathfrak{S}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\theta}^{L}\mathcal{O}_{K}\simeq(\Omega_{(A,M)/\mathcal{O}_{K}})_{p}^{\wedge}

(2)

(crystalline comparison) After scalar extension along the map
\mathfrak{S}\to W(k)
which is the Frobenius on
W(k)
and sends
z
to
0
, there is a functorial Frobenius equivariant isomorphism

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)/\mathfrak{S}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}^{L}W(k)\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}((A_{k},M)/W(k))

Theorem 6.2
.

To any log smooth affine formal log scheme
\operatorname{Spf}(B,N)/(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)
of log Cartier type, one can functorially attach a
(p,z)
-complete
E_{\infty}
-algebra
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(B,N)/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{S})
together with a
\varphi
-linear Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi:{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(B,N)/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)}\to{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(B,N)/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)}
inducing an isomorphism
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(B,N)/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\varphi}\mathfrak{S}[1/E]\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(B,N)/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)}[1/E]
, and having the following features:

(1)

(de Rham comparison) After scalar extension along
\theta
, there is a functorial isomorphism

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(B,N)/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\theta}^{L}\mathcal{O}_{K}\simeq(\Omega_{(B,N)/(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)})^{\wedge}

(2)

(crystalline comparison) After scalar extension along the map
\mathfrak{S}\to W(k)
which is the Frobenius on
W(k)
and sends
z
to
0
, there is a functorial Frobenius equivariant isomorphism

{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(B,N)/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}^{L}W(k)\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}((B_{k},N)/(W(k,\mathbb{N})))

The proof of these theorems will follow the path of
[
BMS19
, Theorem 11.2]
, by first constructing a Frobenius-twisted version. This was indeed first introduced in
[
BLPØ23b
, Theorem 8.1]
, analogously to
[
BMS19
, §11.2]
. Let us briefly recall this construction.

Recall that a
log cyclotomic base
is a pre-log ring spectrum
(R,P)
together with a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{THH}((R,P);\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{THH}((R,P);\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{tC_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{tC_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{p}}

of
S^{1}
-equivariant
E_{\infty}
-rings.
By
[
BMS19
, Proposition 11.3]
(resp. by
[
BLPØ23b
, Lemma 8.5]
) the pre-log ring spectrum
(\mathbb{S}[z],\mathrm{triv})
(resp.
(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle)
) is a log cyclotomic base.
This implies that, for any
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathrm{triv})
-algebra
(A,M)
and for any
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)
-algebra
(B,M^{\prime})
we have cyclotomic structure maps

\displaystyle\varphi\colon{\operatorname{THH}}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{tC_{p}}

\displaystyle\varphi\colon{\operatorname{THH}}((B,M^{\prime})/\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\operatorname{THH}((B,M^{\prime})/\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{tC_{p}}

on relative log topological Hochschild homology
{\operatorname{THH}}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Moreover, by
[
BMS19
, Proposition 11.7]
and
[
BLPØ23b
, Proposition 8.8]
, we have equivalences

(6.2.1)

\operatorname{THH}(\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}],\mathrm{triv})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{THH}(\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}],\langle z^{1/p^{\infty}}\rangle)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

after
p
-completion. These imply that for
(A,M)
a pre-log
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathrm{triv})
-algebra (resp.
(B,N)
a pre-log
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi)
-algebra) we have equivalences

\displaystyle\operatorname{THH}((A,M)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\mathrm{triv}))\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[z]}\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{THH}((A[\varpi^{1/p^{\infty}}],M_{\rm perf})/(\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}],\mathrm{triv}))

\displaystyle\operatorname{THH}((B,N)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle))\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[z]}\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{THH}((B[\varpi^{1/p^{\infty}}],N_{\rm perf})/(\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}],\langle z^{1/p^{\infty}}\rangle)

\displaystyle\operatorname{THH}((A[\varpi^{1/p^{\infty}}],M_{\rm perf})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{THH}((A\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}},M_{\rm perf})

\displaystyle\operatorname{THH}((B[\varpi^{1/p^{\infty}}],N_{\rm perf})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{THH}((B\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}},N_{\rm perf})

after
p
-completion (see
[
BLPØ23b
, Corollary 8.9]
). This implies by
[
BLPØ23b
, Proposition 8.11]
that the presheaves

\displaystyle(S,Q)\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathrm{triv})}

\displaystyle\mapsto\pi_{0}\operatorname{TP}((S,Q)/\mathbb{S}[z],\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle(S,Q)\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\pi_{0}\operatorname{TP}((S,Q)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle),\mathbb{Z}_{p})

are log quasisyntomic sheaves. We define the sheaves of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-
\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}
-algebras

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(-/\mathbb{S}[z];\mathbb{Z}_{p})\colon\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathrm{triv})}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{S})

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(-/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})\colon\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{S})

as the unfolding of
\pi_{0}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(-/\mathbb{S}[z];\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
\pi_{0}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(-/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. We are now ready to state the analogue of
[
BMS19
, Corollary 11.12]
:

Corollary 6.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}=\operatorname{Spf}(A,M)
(resp.
\mathfrak{Y}=\operatorname{Spf}(B,N)
) be an affine log smooth formal scheme over
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathrm{triv})
(resp. an affine log smooth formal scheme over
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)
of log Cartier type). Then the complex
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}}:=\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TC}^{-}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z];\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(resp.
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{Y}/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)^{(-1)}}:=\operatorname{gr}_{0}\operatorname{TC}^{-}((B,N)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle)
) is a
(p,z)
-complete
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-algebra object of
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)})
that admits a natural Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi
and has the following properties:

(1)

There are natural
\varphi
-equivariant equivalences

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}}A_{\rm inf}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})_{(p,z)}^{\wedge}

\displaystyle\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}}}

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{Y}/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)^{(-1)}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}}A_{\rm inf}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})

\displaystyle\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}}}

(2)

There are natural equivalences

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)},\theta^{(-1)}}\mathcal{O}_{K}

\displaystyle\simeq(\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathcal{O}_{K}})_{p}^{\wedge}

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{Y}/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)^{(-1)}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)},\theta^{(-1)}}\mathcal{O}_{K}

\displaystyle\simeq(\Omega_{\mathfrak{Y}/(\mathcal{O}_{K}),\langle\varpi\rangle})_{p}^{\wedge}

of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebras

(3)

After scalar extensions along the map
\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}\to W(k)
which is the identity on
W(k)
and sends
z
to
0
, there are functorial Frobenius equivalence:

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}}W(k)

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(\mathfrak{X}_{k}/W(k))

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{Y}/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)^{(-1)}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}}W(k)

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(\mathfrak{Y}_{k}/W(k,\mathbb{N}))

of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-
W(k)
-algebras

(4)

The Frobenius
\varphi
induces equivalences

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)},\varphi}\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}[1/\varphi(E)]

\displaystyle\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}}[1/\varphi(E)]

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{Y}/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)^{(-1)}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)},\varphi}\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}[1/\varphi(E)]

\displaystyle\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{Y}/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle)^{(-1)}}[1/\varphi(E)]

Proof.

The proof is now completely analogous to
[
BMS19
, Corollary 11.12]
, we report it for completeness. Notice that
\mathcal{O}_{K}\to\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}
is
p
-completely flat, so

\displaystyle(A,M)\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{L}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\simeq(A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}},M)

\displaystyle(B,N)\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty},\langle\varpi^{1/p^{\infty}\rangle}})

\displaystyle\simeq(B\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}},N\oplus_{\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}).

Part
(1)
then follows from the equivalences:

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z],\mathbb{Z}_{p})\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{S}[z]}\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\simeq}\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((A\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}},M),\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((B,N)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle,\mathbb{Z}_{p})\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathbb{S}[z]}\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

\displaystyle\overset{(**)}{\simeq}\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((B\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}},N\oplus_{\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}),\mathbb{Z}_{p})

where
(*)
and
(**)
follow by taking graded pieces and Tate construction on the analogous statement for
\operatorname{THH}
, which follows from the equivalences in (
6.2.1
).
Part
(2)
follows from the equivalences:

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z],\mathbb{Z}_{p})/E

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{HP}((A,M)/\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((B,N)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle,\mathbb{Z}_{p})/E

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{HP}((B,N)/(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle,\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

obtained by unfolding (see also
[
BLPØ23b
, Proof of Theorem 8.1]
), and
[
BLPØ23b
, Theorem 1.3]
. Since
(A,M)
is log smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and the map
\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}[M]
is flat,
\mathcal{O}_{K}\to A
is flat, so
(A,M)\otimes_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathrm{triv})}^{L}k=(A\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}k,M)
is discrete. Moreover, since
\mathbb{N}
is valuative and the map
\mathbb{N}\to N
is injective,
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}]\to\mathbb{Z}[M]
is flat, in particular

\mathcal{O}_{K}\to\mathcal{O}_{K}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}]}\mathbb{Z}[N]\to B

is flat, so
(B,N)\otimes_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)}^{L}(k,\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})=(B\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}k,N)
is discrete too. Part
(2)
follows from

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z])\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[z]}\mathbb{S}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((A,M)\otimes_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathrm{triv})}k)\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A/\varpi,M)}

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((B,N)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle))\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[z]}^{L}\mathbb{S}

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\simeq}\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((B,N)\otimes_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)}(k,\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}))\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(B/\varpi,N\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})},

where
(*)
follows from the equivalence after
p
-completion
\operatorname{THH}(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})\to\mathbb{S}
, proved in the same way as
[
BLPØ23b
, Proposition 8.8]
using Proposition
4.7
. Then Part
(3)
follows now from (
4.30.1
) and (
4.31.1
). Finally, Part
(4)
follows from base change via the topological direct summand
\mathfrak{S}^{(-1)}\to A_{\rm inf}(\mathcal{O}_{C})
and
(1)
, so similarly to
[
BMS19
, Corollary 11.12 (4)]
it is enough to show that
\varphi^{*}{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}/\mathcal{O}_{C}}\to{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}/\mathcal{O}_{C}}
induces an equivalence after inverting
\tilde{\xi}
: since
\mathfrak{X}
is assumed to be log smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, we have that
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}/\mathcal{O}_{C}}\simeq{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\rm nc}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}/\mathcal{O}_{C}}
, and by Corollary
4.22
we reduce to the same question for the non-logarithmic non-Nygaard completed prismatic cohomology of
[
BMS19
, Construction 7.12]
, which in turns is equivalent by left Kan extension and
[
BMS19
, Theorem 9.6]
to the derived
A\Omega
-cohomology, for which the result follows by left Kan extension on
p
-completely smooth
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebras.
∎

We are now ready to prove our version of the Frobenius descent, following
[
BMS19
, Proposition 11.15]
:

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
(A,M)
be an
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathrm{triv})
-pre-log algebra (resp. (B,N) an
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)
-pre-log algebra).

(1)

The cyclotomic Frobenii

\displaystyle\operatorname{TC}^{-}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z];\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varphi}\operatorname{TP}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z];\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\operatorname{TC}^{-}((B,N)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varphi}\operatorname{TP}((B,N)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})

extend naturally maps

\displaystyle\operatorname{TC}^{-}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z]);\mathbb{Z}_{p})[1/u]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[z]}\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{TP}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z];\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\operatorname{TC}^{-}((B,N)/({\mathbb{S}}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})[1/u]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[z]}\mathbb{S}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{TP}((B,M)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

(2)

On log quasiregular semiperfectoids, the source of the above map is concentrated in even degrees, and the presheaves

\displaystyle(A,M)\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\pi_{0}\operatorname{TC}^{-}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z]);\mathbb{Z}_{p})[1/u]

\displaystyle(B,N)\in\operatorname{lQRSPerfd}_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\pi_{0}\operatorname{TC}^{-}((B,N)/({\mathbb{S}}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})[1/u]

are sheaves with vanishing cohomology.

(3)

The unfoldings
{\operatorname{gr}}^{0}
on
\operatorname{lQSyn}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
and
\operatorname{lQSyn}_{(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)}
give natural maps

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TC}^{-}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z]);\mathbb{Z}_{p})[1/u]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[z]}\mathbb{Z}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z];\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TC}^{-}((B,N)/({\mathbb{S}}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})[1/u]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[z]}\mathbb{Z}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{TP}((B,N)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

resulting from functoriality of unfolding. The maps above are equivalences if
(A,M)
is the
p
-adic completion of a log smooth
({\mathcal{O}}_{K},\mathrm{triv})
-algebra, resp. if
(B,N)
is the
p
-adic completion of a log smooth
({\mathcal{O}}_{K},\langle\bar{\omega}\rangle)
-algebra of log Cartier type.

Proof.

Observe first that the map
({\mathbb{S}}[z],\langle z\rangle)\to({\mathcal{O}}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)
is strict, so

\operatorname{THH}(\mathcal{O}_{K},\langle\varpi\rangle)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle)\simeq\operatorname{THH}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathbb{S}[z]).

Part
(1)
now follows like in
[
BMS19
, Proposition 11.15 (1)]
from the fact that the Frobenius in this case maps
u
to
\sigma
and the map is linear over
\mathbb{S}[z]\to\mathbb{S}[z]
,
z\mapsto z^{p}
.

Part
(2)
follows again from the analogous statement with
u
not inverted and by passage to filtered colimits. To prove part
(3)
, as in
[
BMS19
, Proof of Proposition 11.10]
we can show that for
i\gg 0
we have a equivalences

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TC}^{-}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z]);\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[z]}\mathbb{Z}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TP}((A,M)/\mathbb{S}[z];\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TC}^{-}((B,N)/({\mathbb{S}}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[z]}\mathbb{Z}[z^{1/p^{\infty}}]

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TP}((B,N)/(\mathbb{S}[z],\langle z\rangle);\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

By reducing modulo
z^{1/p}
and using the equivalences

\mathbb{S}_{p}^{\wedge}\simeq\operatorname{THH}(\mathbb{S};\mathbb{Z}_{p})\simeq\operatorname{THH}((\mathbb{S},\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf},\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

we reduce to show that for any
(\overline{A},M)
log smooth over
k
and any
(\overline{B},N)
log smooth over
(k,\mathbb{N})
with
\mathbb{N}\to N
saturates, the maps

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(\overline{A},M))

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TP}(\overline{A},M)

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TC}^{-}(\overline{B},N\oplus{N}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf})

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{gr}^{i}\operatorname{TP}(\overline{B},N\oplus_{\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{N}_{\rm perf}),

and this follows respectively from Propositions
5.1
and
5.2
, in place of
[
BMS19
, Corollary 8.18]
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.1
and
6.2
.

We define
\widehat{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(A,M)/\mathfrak{S}}:=\operatorname{gr}^{0}(\operatorname{TC}^{-}((A,M)/({\mathbb{S}}[z]);\mathbb{Z}_{p})[1/u])
and
\widehat{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(B,N)/(\mathfrak{S},\langle z\rangle}):=\operatorname{gr}^{0}(\operatorname{TC}^{-}((A,M)/({\mathbb{S}}[z]);\mathbb{Z}_{p})[1/u])
. A
verbatim
translation of the argument of
[
BMS19
, Proof of Theorem 11.2]
applies to conclude: replace the applications of
[
BMS19
, Proposition 11.15, Corollary 11.12]
with Proposition
6.4
and Corollary
6.3
, respectively.
∎

We finish this section by showing that the cohomology
{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-/\mathfrak{S}}
gives rise to a motivic spectrum in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathfrak{S})
. Let
\mathfrak{S}\{-1\}:=H^{2}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}/\mathfrak{S})
(see
[
BL22
, Variant 9.1.6]
for the bounded prism
(\mathfrak{S},E(z))
): it is an invertible
\mathfrak{S}
-module and by the crystalline comparison we get a canonical trivialization
\mathfrak{S}\{-1\}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}^{L}W(k)\simeq W(k)
as
W(k)
-modules. Let

R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/\mathfrak{S})\{i\}:=R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(-/\mathfrak{S})\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\mathfrak{S}}\mathfrak{S}\{-1\}^{\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}-i}.

As in Construction
3.12
, consider the prismatic Chern class
c_{1}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathcal{O}(1))
considered in
[
BL22
, Variant 9.1.6]
. Then, similarly to (
3.12.3
), for all
n,i\in\mathbb{Z}
we have functorially in
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
a map in
\widehat{\mathcal{D}\mathcal{F}(\mathfrak{S})}
:

R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S})\{n-i\}[-2i]\to R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E})/\mathfrak{S})\{n\}.

Since
R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S})\{n-i\}
is
(p,z)
-complete, it is in particular
z
-complete, so since the projection maps

(6.4.1)

R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}\times_{\operatorname{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}(\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})/\mathfrak{S})\{n-i\}\to R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S})\{n-i\}

coincide mod
z
(i.e. by applying
-\otimes^{L}_{\mathfrak{S}}W(k)
) with the maps

R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(\mathfrak{X}_{k}\times_{k}(\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})/W(k))\to R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(\mathfrak{X}_{k}/W(k)),

which are equivalences by
[
Mer22
, Theorem 1.3]
, so (
6.4.1
) is an equivalence, implying that for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
we have graded commutative monoids in
\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(R,\mathfrak{S})
:

E_{*}^{\mathrm{BK}}:=\{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{-/\mathfrak{S}}\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}^{L}_{\mathfrak{S}}\mathfrak{S}\{-1\}^{\operatorname{\widehat{\otimes}}^{L}_{\mathfrak{S}}-i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}},

where the completion is the
(p,z)
-completion.

We are left to show that the map

R\Gamma_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S})\{i\}\to\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}/\mathfrak{S})\{i+1\}[2].

induced by the prismatic Chern class is an equivalence.

We argue as follows. For
{\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}
a smooth formal scheme with trivial log structure, the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}/\mathfrak{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}/\mathfrak{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}/\mathfrak{S})\otimes^{L}_{\mathfrak{S}}W(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}_{k}/W(k))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}_{k}/W(k)),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the left-hand side is the absolute prismatic cohomology of
[
BL22
]
and the vertical map is the specialization, so, in particular, the prismatic Chern class of
R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}/\mathfrak{S})
agrees modulo
z
with the crystalline Chern class of
R\Gamma_{\rm crys}({\underline{\mathfrak{X}}}_{k}/W(k))
(using
[
BL22
, Proposition 7.5.5]
again).

We can then reduce again to the case of the crystalline cohomology by
z
-completeness of
[
Mer22
, (4.5.1)]
.
Finally, the equivalences of Theorem
6.1
induce equivalences of graded commutative monoids:

\displaystyle E_{*}^{\mathrm{BK}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\theta}^{L}\mathcal{O}_{K}

\displaystyle\simeq(E_{*}^{\mathrm{dR}})_{p}^{\wedge}

\displaystyle\mathrm{in}\ \mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{Z}^{\rm ds},\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathcal{O}_{K}))

\displaystyle E_{*}^{\mathrm{BK}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}^{L}W(k)

\displaystyle\simeq(i_{*}E_{*}^{\mathrm{crys}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{in}\ \mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{Z}^{\rm ds},\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{{\operatorname{eff}}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},W(k))),

where
i_{*}E_{n}^{\rm crys}(\mathfrak{X}):=E_{n}^{\rm crys}(\mathfrak{X}_{k})=R\Gamma_{\rm crys}(\mathfrak{X}_{k})
, with Chern classes that agree with de Rham and crystalline Chern classes respectively again by
[
BL22
]
.
By
2.16
and
2.17
, we conclude that

Theorem 6.5
.

There is an oriented ring spectrum
\mathbf{E}^{\rm BK}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathfrak{S}))
such that for all
\mathfrak{X}\in\operatorname{\mathrm{FlSm}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
we have

\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathfrak{S})}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{\rm BK})\simeq R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{S})\{s\}[r].

Moreover, there are equivalences of oriented ring spectra:

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{BK}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\theta}^{L}\mathcal{O}_{K}

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{dR}})_{p}^{\wedge}

\displaystyle\mathrm{in}\ \operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathcal{O}_{K})

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{BK}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}^{L}W(k)

\displaystyle\simeq(i_{*}\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{crys}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{in}\ \operatorname{\mathbf{log}\mathcal{FDA}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},W(k)).

Again we deduce from motivic properties similar results to Theorem
3.17
, Theorem
3.21
and Theorem
3.22
, which after base change agree with the usual properties of de Rham and crystalline cohomology. This has the following interesting application:

Corollary 6.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and
Z\subseteq X
a closed subscheme of relative codimension
d
, such that
Z\to X\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is smooth. Let
{\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X)
denote the blow-up of
X
in
Z
and
E
be the exceptional divisor, so that
({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)\in\operatorname{\mathrm{SmlSm}}_{R}
. Then for all
j
there is a Gysin map in
\mathcal{D}(A_{\inf})

R\Gamma_{A_{\inf}}((Z_{p}^{\wedge})_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})\{j-d\}[-2d]\to R\Gamma_{A_{\inf}}((X_{p}^{\wedge})_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})\{j\}

whose homotopy cofiber is

R\Gamma_{{\widehat{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X),E)_{p}^{\wedge}/\mathfrak{S})\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}A_{\inf}.

There is an equivalence, functorial in
(X,Z)
,

R\Gamma_{A_{\inf}}(X^{\wedge}_{p})\{j\}\oplus\bigoplus_{0<i<d}R\Gamma_{A_{\inf}}(Z^{\wedge}_{p})\{j-i\}[-2i]\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma_{A_{\inf}}(({\mathbf{Bl}}_{Z}(X))^{\wedge}_{p})\{j\}

For
X\in\operatorname{\mathrm{lSm}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
, we have an equivalence of bigraded rings:

\varphi^{\rm BK}_{n,r}\colon H^{*}_{A_{\inf}}((X_{p}^{\wedge})_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})\{\bullet\}\otimes{Z}R_{n,r}\xrightarrow{\simeq}H^{*}_{A_{\inf}}((\mathrm{Gr}(n,r)\times X)_{p}^{\wedge})_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})\{\bullet\}

Proof.

Apply (
2.18.2
), (
2.19.4
) and
[
BPØ23
, Theorem 7.4.6]
to
\mathbf{E}^{\rm BK}
and then
-\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}A_{\inf}
: the result follows from
[
BMS19
, Theorem 1.2 (1)]
.
∎

Remark 6.7
.

An
A_{\inf}
-cohomology theory for general logarithmic
p
-adic formal schemes has been constructed by Diao and Yao in
[
DY23
]
, building on the construction of Česnavičius and Koshikawa in
[
ČK19
]
for the semistable case. We expect that an analogue of
[
BMS19
, Theorem 1.2 (1)]
can be applied to compare our Breuil–Kisin cohomologies with these
A_{\inf}
-cohomologies.
